## v0.28 — Traction control on a measured slip target (`tc_slip` 2.5 m/s, `tc_gain` 0.5)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.28 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.28`). The traction control is rebuilt (lines 634–644), with two new knobs, `tc_slip=2.5` (line 551) and `tc_gain=.5` (line 552). Before: when the rear wheels turned more than 5 rad/s (summed) faster than the fronts, 0.2 was taken off the stored throttle, which then climbed back at +0.05 per step. Now: rear over-speed is measured in m/s, `(rear wheels × 0.315 m) − (front wheels × 0.302 m)` (tyre radii from `car1-ow1.xml`); beyond `tc_slip` the throttle sent is cut by `tc_gain` per m/s over. The cut is applied only to the value sent: the stored throttle (`c.throttle`, restored at the start of each step, line 554) keeps its value, so the next step starts from where it was. Plan item D, first step (traction control; the throttle itself is a separate version). Telemetry: new column `throttle` (stored throttle before the cut; `accel` is what was sent). |
| **Why** | The user sees a lot of wheelspin out of corners. Our own measurement, from the v0.22–v0.27 laps (steps with throttle ≥ 0.5, no brake, `\|steer\|` ≤ 0.3, no gear change): acceleration rises with rear over-speed up to **2–2.5 m/s** and then falls — 2nd gear 8.6 m/s² at 1.5 m/s, 9.6 at 2.0, 9.5 at 2.5; 3rd 8.0 at 1.5, 10.2 at 2.0, 10.9 at 2.5, 10.7 at 3.0, 8.5 at 3.5; 4th 9.1 at 2.0, 8.4 at 2.5. Sideways speed only starts to build above ~2.5 m/s (change in `\|speedY\|` per step −0.01 at 2.0, +0.06 at 2.5, +0.12 at 3.0, +0.32 km/h at 3.5). The old rad/s threshold works out to 1.5 m/s at 60 km/h, 2.0 at 100, 2.6 at 150 and 3.2 at 200 km/h (the smaller front tyre spins faster), so in the low gears it cut before the acceleration peak. And because the −0.2 came off the stored throttle, the throttle stayed below its pre-cut value for 18.4% of the v0.27 lap. A proportional cut is a known idea (JM Par has one too); the target and gain here come from our own data. |
| **Prediction** | Replay with the real `drive_example()` on every logged v0.27 row: steering, brake and gear identical in all 4,434 steps; throttle sent differs in 319 steps — mostly +0.2 (no fixed cut below 2.5 m/s), lower in 42 heavy-spin steps (by up to 0.44). Driven, the throttle should stay open on exits: traction-control cuts (−0.15 or more in one step without braking) well below 207, full throttle above 17% of the lap, rear over-speed mostly 2–2.5 m/s under power. Gains on corner exits and the short straights (~600, ~1,200, ~1,600, ~2,000, ~2,600 m), lap roughly 0.3–1.0 s faster than 1:32.30. Risks: the 2.5 m/s limit was measured mostly with little steering (mean `\|steer\|` 0.06 at those slips), so exits with steering may slide more — watch sideways speed at ~2,000 m (6.5 km/h), ~2,360 m (7.4), the flick (11.0) and the hairpin exit (`\|trackPos\|` 0.50); wheelspin right after upshifts (~1,600 m); slip-raised rpm causing extra shifts. Reject on damage or leaving the track. Damage expected to stay at 0. |
| **Lap time** | 1:31.24 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 212 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 73 km/h |
| **Observed** | Car completed a full lap with zero damage and the damage stop never triggered. Lap time 1.06 s faster than v0.27 (1:32.30 → 1:31.24) — the fastest lap so far, at the top of the predicted 0.3–1.0 s. Top speed 212 km/h. Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_184552.csv`): gains at the start (−0.20 s, launch), ~1,900 m (−0.16 s), the flick (~2,400 m −0.14 s, ~2,500 m −0.12 s), ~3,300 m (−0.08 s), ~500 m (−0.07 s), ~800, ~1,000, ~3,000 and ~3,400 m (−0.05 s each); small losses at ~1,500 m (+0.04 s) and ~1,600, ~1,800, ~2,000, ~2,200, ~2,900 m (+0.02 s each). Full throttle 17% → 27% of the lap; at full stored throttle the rear over-speed has a median of 1.98 m/s (90th percentile 3.65). **The cut chatters:** because it is released at once, the throttle sent jumps between ~0 and 1 under sustained spin (flick ~2,471–2,480 m: 0.02, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0.60, 0.58, 1.00, 0.45, 0.65, 1.00; hairpin exit ~3,288–3,320 m: 0.1–0.6 at 3.5–4.4 m/s over-speed). The throttle sent was below the stored throttle in 617 steps (14.2% of the lap), by 0.15 or more in 397 — the predicted drop in cuts did not happen. Rear spin > 5 rad/s 7.6% → 18.3%. **Near miss at the ~3,283 m hairpin:** at full lock for ~30 m (~3,263–3,288 m) the stored throttle climbed to 1.0 (v0.27, `\|steer\|` > 0.6: throttle sent mean 0.20, max 0.40; v0.28: mean 0.27, max 1.00); the car held 73–75 km/h (minimum 68 in v0.27) and ran wide from `trackPos` +0.07 to **−0.92** at ~3,292 m, 0.08 from the edge, and started the next straight at −0.9 (max `\|trackPos\|` 0.50 in v0.27). **Biggest slide so far at the flick:** at full lock (~2,466–2,471 m) the throttle went to 1.00 with the rear at 2.0–2.4 m/s (below the limit), speed rose 74 → 82 km/h and `trackPos` −0.18 → −0.61; then the rear spun at 4.5–6.2 m/s, and at the direction change (~2,486–2,497 m) sideways speed reached **24.0 km/h** (11.0 in v0.27). Medium corners slid 1–3 km/h more (~500 m 4.4 → 7.8, ~1,900 m 5.9 → 8.4, ~400 m 4.7 → 6.8 km/h). Slowest corner 68 → 73 km/h (~3,266 m). Steering reversals 63 → 71, gear changes 50 → 62, braking 31% of the lap. |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — 1.06 s faster, zero damage and the car stayed on the track; but the hairpin exit at `trackPos` −0.92 leaves almost no margin, so v0.29 fixes that before anything else |
| **Learned** | The slip target measured on our laps pays on straights and in gentle corners (full throttle 17% → 27%), but it was measured with almost no steering. At full lock the tyres have no grip to spare: throttle there runs the car wide (hairpin) or makes it slide (flick). The old traction control had hidden this by accident — its threshold was ~1.5 m/s at 74 km/h and its lasting cuts held the throttle near 0.2 at full lock. Releasing the cut at once makes the throttle chatter on and off under sustained spin; a smoother release is a candidate for later. |

## v0.29 — Throttle limit near full lock (`lock_steer` 0.6, `lock_throttle` 0.3)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.29 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.29`). New knobs `lock_steer=.6` (line 553) and `lock_throttle=.3` (line 554) and a new block before the traction control (lines 636–643): above `\|steer\|` 0.6 the throttle is limited, the limit falling linearly from 1 to 0.3 at full lock. The limit applies to the stored throttle as well as the one sent, so the throttle cannot wind up to 1.0 during the lock and snap open as the wheel straightens; afterwards it climbs back at +0.05 per step. Traction control (`tc_slip` 2.5, `tc_gain` 0.5) is unchanged. |
| **Why** | Safety fix for v0.28. At full lock the car is already turning as tight as it can, so extra speed only pushes it wide. v0.28 let the throttle reach 1.0 at full lock: at the ~3,283 m hairpin the car ran out to `trackPos` −0.92 (0.08 from the edge), and in the flick it gained 74 → 82 km/h at full lock and then slid at 24 km/h. In v0.27 the throttle at `\|steer\|` > 0.6 averaged ~0.2 (max 0.40) with `\|trackPos\|` ≤ 0.50 and 11 km/h at the flick, so 0.3 at full lock is close to that, safe level. Only the flick and the hairpin pass 0.6 (~1,926 m peaks at 0.62). Our own idea: the steering angle shows when the tyres have no grip left for power. |
| **Prediction** | Replay with the real `drive_example()` on every logged v0.28 row: steering, brake and gear identical in all 4,385 steps; the throttle differs only where `\|steer\|` > 0.66 — flick 24 steps (throttle sent mean 0.64 → 0.31), hairpin 35 steps (0.53 → 0.24); nowhere else. Driven: hairpin exit `\|trackPos\|` back to ~0.5–0.6, flick sideways speed back toward ~11–15 km/h, speeds in both corners close to v0.27's (68–74 km/h). Part of v0.28's gain there may be given back (flick and ~3,300 m gained ~0.3 s), so the lap should land roughly between 1:31.2 and 1:31.6. If it is a little slower but the margin is restored, it would be kept as an enabling change: a safe hairpin and flick are needed before raising `turn_grip` or the throttle. Risks: slower drive out of the hairpin onto the ~3,300 m straight. Reject on damage or leaving the track. Damage expected to stay at 0. |
| **Lap time** | 1:31.26 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 213 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 73 km/h |
| **Observed** | Car completed a full lap with zero damage and the damage stop never triggered. Lap time 0.02 s slower than v0.28 (1:31.24 → 1:31.26), inside the predicted 1:31.2–1:31.6. Top speed 213 km/h. Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_185419.csv`): identical to v0.28 up to ~2,400 m, as the replay predicted. **Flick calmer:** sideways speed 24.0 → 16.2 km/h (~2,400 m section) and 17.4 → 10.7 km/h (~2,500 m), max `\|trackPos\|` 0.61 → 0.54; section times +0.06 s and −0.02 s. **Hairpin exit barely changed:** `\|trackPos\|` 0.92 → 0.86 (~3,290 m). At full lock (~3,263–3,288 m) the throttle sat at the 0.30 cap and the car held 73.1–73.6 km/h while drifting from −0.15 to −0.86; in v0.27 the throttle chopped between 0.15 and 0.35 and the car slowed to 68.3 km/h and stayed at −0.50. Other sections within ±0.05 s (~2,700 m −0.05 s, ~2,600 and ~3,000 m +0.03 s). Slowest corner 73 km/h (~3,266 m). Full throttle 26%, braking 30%, rear spin > 5 rad/s 18.6%. **Racing line (user's screenshot at ~2,926 m, 136 km/h):** the car is in the centre (`trackPos` −0.01) on the approach to the ~2,973 m right-hander. The line only starts when `aim` passes 2°, here at ~2,935 m with 36 m of road left, so its phase is already "inside"; it then moves the car only to −0.26. The beam tilt (`track11` − `track7`) reads "right bend" (+0.07) from ~2,803 m, 170 m before. Track width measured from the ±45° beams: 12.0 m (`trackPos` ±1 = ±6 m). Line active 48% of the lap, mean `\|trackPos\|` 0.15, above 0.5 only 4.5% of the lap. |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — enabling change: 0.02 s slower, but the flick slide fell 24 → 16 km/h; the hairpin exit (0.86) still needs work |
| **Learned** | Capping the throttle at full lock limits acceleration but does not slow the car: at 0.30 the car holds 73 km/h in the hairpin, which needs ~68. The hairpin needs either a lower `lock_throttle` (v0.27 averaged ~0.2) or a speed plan that knows the hairpin is tighter than the 75 km/h `corner_speed` floor. Kept as an enabling change because the flick, the corner that has limited corner speed since v0.11, is clearly calmer. The racing line barely uses the 12 m track because it starts too late; the beam tilt shows the turn side ~170 m earlier. |

## v0.30 — Racing line from the beam tilt, with rate-limited moves (`line_range` 150, `line_rate` 0.008, `line_rate_in` 0.025)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.30 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.30`). The racing-line target is rebuilt (lines 580–610), with new knobs `line_range=150` (line 544), `line_rate=.008` (line 545) and `line_rate_in=.025` (line 546). Before: the line worked only while the look-ahead bearing `aim` was over 2°, with the side from the sign of `aim`. Now: it works as soon as a corner is in view (`ahead` < 150 m); the turn side comes from the tilt of the far road edge, `(track[11] − track[7]) / ahead` (+1° vs −1° beams), updated when over 0.02, held otherwise and reset when no corner is in view. The phase (outside at `ahead` ≥ 80 m, inside at ≤ 40 m), `line_offset` 0.5 and `line_gain` 0.50 are unchanged. New: the target moves at most 0.008 `trackPos` per metre driven toward the outside or back to the centre, and 0.025 per metre toward the inside; it is kept between steps. `lineTarget` in the CSV is this rate-limited target. Plan item C, first step. |
| **Why** | The user wants to use the track width (screenshot at ~2,926 m: car in the centre before a right-hander). v0.29 data: the line starts only ~40 m before a corner, when its phase is already "inside", so the car never goes to the outside (mean `\|trackPos\|` 0.15 on a 12 m track); the beam tilt gives the turn side ~170 m before (+0.07 from ~2,803 m for the ~2,973 m corner). v0.25 used the same tilt trigger and was rejected because the target snapped: the car's nose turned toward the edge at 199 km/h, the nose beams saw a false end of the road and the plan braked on a straight. v0.26 has since added the look-ahead along the track direction for angles within 3°; the outward rate limit keeps the car's angle under ~3° (0.008 × 6 m half-width ≈ 0.048 m per m ≈ 2.7°), so that look-ahead keeps working. The inward limit (1.0 in ~40 m) is fast enough for turn-in but stops the target jumping when the turn side flips mid-flick. |
| **Prediction** | Replay of the target on the v0.29 lap: on the outside 80–120 m before most corners (e.g. ~2,973 m: +0.44 at 80 m out, −0.27 at 50 m, −0.50 at the apex; ~422 m: −0.50 at 80 m; ~1,523 m: −0.38 at 120 m) and on the inside near each apex; the flick's side change becomes a 40 m ramp (+0.44 → −0.05 → −0.50) instead of a jump; active 89% of the lap, 22 sign changes. Real `drive_example()` on every logged v0.29 row (target state carried between steps): steering differs in 2,597 of 4,386 steps (mean 0.07, max 0.40), brake in 1, throttle in 54; gears identical. Driven: `\|trackPos\|` up to ~0.4–0.5 on corner approaches (it settles near 0.42 for a 0.5 target), mean `\|trackPos\|` well above 0.15; wider entries, so the sharpness plan sees longer beams and corner minimums rise a few km/h; lap roughly 0.3–1.5 s faster than 1:31.26. Risks: a false short `ahead` on fast approaches if the car angles past 3° (watch `aheadPlan` drops and braking on straights, e.g. ~2,228 m as in v0.25); busier steering; the ~3,283 m hairpin now starts from the right-hand outside and its exit is already at −0.86 — reject if the car leaves the track; the flick (16 km/h sideways). Reject on damage or leaving the track. Damage expected to stay at 0. |
| **Lap time** | 1:31.52 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 213 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 73 km/h |
| **Observed** | Car completed a full lap with zero damage, but **it left the track**: at the ~3,283 m hairpin exit `\|trackPos\|` passed 0.95 for 19 steps (~3,287–3,295 m) and reached **1.009** (~3,290 m; all beams read −1 for a moment). Lap time 0.26 s **slower** than v0.29 (1:31.26 → 1:31.52). Top speed 213 km/h. Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_190312.csv`): **the car did not follow the line.** Approaching the ~2,973 m right-hander the target rose as planned (+0.07 at ~2,833 m to +0.47 at ~2,889 m), but the car only moved from +0.12 to +0.18, with the steering at ~0.00: the line's pull (`(target − trackPos)·0.50` ≈ +0.14) was cancelled by the heading term (`angle·15/π`, car angled −0.017 rad ≈ −0.08), the centring term and the look-ahead term. In this balance the car closes the gap to the line with a lag of ~57 m (6 m half-width ÷ (0.50 ÷ 4.77 per rad)). Mean `\|trackPos\|` 0.145 → 0.139, above 0.3 9.5% → 6.6% of the lap. **The inside switch came too early:** the phase turns the target inside as `ahead` falls from 80 to 40 m, which at ~2,973 m is ~2,889–2,935 m, while the car turns in (`aim` > 2°) at ~2,939 m, so it drifted from +0.20 to 0.00 toward the inside *before* turn-in (an early apex). At the hairpin the target was inside (+0.50) from ~3,229 m (`ahead` 39 m), turn-in at ~3,251 m (`ahead` 18 m); the car entered from +0.15 (v0.29 +0.13) at 75 km/h (v0.29 73.1–73.6), held full lock and ran out to −1.01. Section losses: ~3,300 m +0.08 s, flick +0.06/+0.08 s, ~800 and ~2,700 m +0.07 s; gain ~700 m −0.05 s. Flick sideways speed 16.2 → 19.7 km/h. Steering reversals 67 → 15 (the smooth target calmed the steering). Slowest corner 73 km/h (~2,493 m). |
| **Decision** | ❌ Rejected — left the track at the hairpin exit (`trackPos` −1.009) and 0.26 s slower. Driver code and tuning card reverted to v0.29 (`git checkout v0.29 -- snakeoil3_v1.py tuning-card.md`). |
| **Learned** | Two separate problems keep the line from working. (1) **The pull is too weak:** the heading term (`angle·15/π`) balances any line pull with a small angle, so the car moves toward the line with a ~57 m lag and never reaches the outside; a stronger pull is needed, but with the car's angle kept under ~3° so the look-ahead keeps working (e.g. limit the line's steering, not the target). (2) **The inside switch must wait for the turn-in:** the visible road ahead falls below 60 m long before the car turns in, so a phase based on `ahead` gives early apexes — a bad entry and a bad exit (the user's point). `aim` passing 2° marks the actual turn-in well (~2,939 m, ~3,251 m). The hairpin is fragile: 1–2 km/h more at full lock (75 vs 73) moves the exit from −0.86 to −1.01, so its speed must come down before the line can be pushed there. |

In v0.27 the throttle at full lock averaged ~0.2 (chopped between 0.15 and 0.35), the car slowed to 68.3 km/h and exited at −0.50. |
| **Prediction** | Replay with the real `drive_example()` on every logged v0.29 row: steering, brake and gear identical; the throttle differs only where `\|steer\|` > 0.69 — hairpin 67 steps (throttle sent mean 0.30 → 0.20), flick 35 steps (0.32 → 0.23); nowhere else. Driven: the car slows at full lock in the hairpin toward ~68–70 km/h and its exit comes in to roughly −0.5 to −0.7; flick sideways speed ≤ 16 km/h. Lap time about the same as v0.29 (1:31.26), within ~±0.15 s — slower through the hairpin and flick, possibly faster on the exits if the car is better placed. If a little slower, it would be kept as an enabling change: a safe hairpin is needed before the racing line is reworked. Reject on damage or leaving the track. Damage expected to stay at 0. |
| **Lap time** | 1:31.37 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 213 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 66 km/h |

Top speed 213 km/h. Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_191013.csv`): identical to v0.29 except at the flick and the hairpin, as the replay predicted. **Hairpin exit has margin again:** minimum speed 73 → 66 km/h (~3,285 m), exit `\|trackPos\|` 0.86 → **0.59**; section times ~3,200 m +0.04 s, ~3,300 m +0.02 s. **Flick calmer:** sideways speed 16.2 → 14.0 km/h (~2,400 m section) and 10.7 → 7.6 km/h (~2,500 m); +0.02 and +0.03 s. Max `\|trackPos\|` 0.59. Full throttle 26%, braking 30%, rear spin > 5 rad/s 17.0%. **Sensors (asked by the user):** the SCR server computes each track beam in 2D on the track's segment map (`scr_server/sensors.cpp`: straight and arc segments, from the car's distance to the left edge and along the segment; no elevation), so the beams see over crests and down the Corkscrew drop, up to their 200 m range. The car (`car1-ow1`) is 2.4 m wide on the 12 m track: its outer wheels reach the edge at `\|trackPos\|` 0.8. |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — enabling change (the user chose the hairpin first): 0.11 s slower, but the hairpin exit came in from −0.86 to −0.59, which the racing-line rework needs |
| **Learned** | At full lock the hairpin speed sets the exit width directly: 66 km/h → −0.59, 73 → −0.86, 75 → −1.01 (off). The margin costs ~0.1 s; a better entry line (larger radius) should win it back. The beams are track-map geometry, not line of sight, so a corner can be planned for as soon as it is within 200 m, crest or not. |

## v0.32 — Racing line: outside from 200 m, inside only after turn-in, stronger capped pull (`line_offset` 0.85, `line_gain` 1.5, `line_range` 200, `line_steer_max` 0.21)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.32 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.32`). The racing line is rebuilt (lines 579–605); `line_offset` 0.5 → 0.85 (line 542), `line_gain` 0.50 → 1.5 (line 543), new `line_range=200` (line 544) and `line_steer_max=.21` (line 545). Before: the line worked only while the look-ahead bearing `aim` was over 2°, outside/inside by `ahead` (≥ 80 m / ≤ 40 m). Now: (1) as soon as the end of the road is in view (`ahead` < 200 m) the target is the **outside** of the coming corner, the side from the beam tilt `(track[11] − track[7]) / ahead` (> 0.02, held otherwise, reset when `ahead` reaches 200 m); (2) only once the car turns in (`\|aim\|` > 2°) does it use the old phase (inside when ≤ 40 m of road is visible, back out on the exit), side from `aim`; (3) the pull `(target − trackPos)·1.5` is capped at ±0.21 steer. |
| **Why** | The user wants the line to use the whole track ("if we see an incoming left turn on a straight, move as far right as possible"; "don't take the 0.8 limit that seriously"; "don't prioritise keeping the car parallel if the line is better") — a bad entry gives a bad exit. v0.30's lessons: the pull at 0.50 is cancelled by the heading term (~57 m lag), and the `ahead`-based phase turned inside ~30 m before the turn-in (early apex); `aim` > 2° marks the real turn-in (~2,939 m, ~3,251 m). The beams see over crests (2D track map), so the line can start as soon as any end of road is within the 200 m range. The cap of 0.21 is the steering the heading term balances at ~2.5° of car angle, so on approaches the car stays under the 3° within which `ahead` is measured along the track direction — above it v0.25 saw a false end of road and braked on a straight. 0.85 puts the outer wheels ~0.3 m over the edge (car 2.4 m wide, edge at 0.8). |
| **Prediction** | Replay with the real `drive_example()` on every logged v0.31 row (line state carried): steering differs in 3,799 of 4,391 steps (mean 0.11, max 0.32), brake in 4, throttle in 147; gears identical. Target at the ~2,973 m corner: +0.85 (outside) until turn-in at ~2,938 m, then −0.85; hairpin: −0.85 from ~3,150 m, +0.85 after turn-in at ~3,239 m. A rough lateral simulation (car angle = line steer ÷ 4.77, max ~0.0073 trackPos per metre) puts the car at ~0.8–0.85 on the outside at turn-in after the long approaches (~395, ~976, ~1,221, ~1,472, ~1,907, ~2,935, ~3,235 and ~3,568 m), only part-way before close corners (~579 m +0.18, ~721 m +0.45, ~2,003 m −0.47, ~2,334 m +0.16, flick −0.48). Wider entries → the sharpness plan sees longer beams → higher corner speeds; lap roughly 0.5–2 s faster than 1:31.37. Risks: `\|trackPos\|` will run up to ~0.85 by design — any `\|trackPos\|` > 1 rejects; the hairpin now starts from the right edge and its exit runs right (−0.59 in v0.31); the flick; busier steering; the line's pull in corners is now capped at 0.21 (uncapped before). Reject on damage or leaving the track. Damage expected to stay at 0. |
| **Lap time** | 1:32.45 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 212 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 72 km/h |
| **Observed** | Car completed a full lap with zero damage and stayed on track (max `\|trackPos\|` 0.588 at ~355 m), but it was **1.08 s slower** than v0.31 (1:31.37 → 1:32.45). Top speed 212 km/h. The user saw the wheel and steering oscillate a lot. Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_191933.csv`): **the car still did not reach the line.** On the longest approach (target −0.85 from ~3,076 m) it got to only −0.56 by ~3,200 m; elsewhere 0.3–0.57 (~976 m +0.45, ~3,568 m −0.57). With the line's steering capped at 0.21 the car settles at ~1.5° of angle, ~0.23 `trackPos` per second. Mean `\|trackPos\|` 0.13 → 0.23 (above 0.3: 8% → 31% of the lap). **The target oscillated in corners:** it jumped by more than 0.2 in 261 steps (v0.31 81), because `\|aim\|` hovers around 2° (142 crossings per lap, v0.31 64), flipping the side and the outside/inside switch, and the phase comes from the nose beams, which the line's own steering swings (at ~1,190–1,260 m `ahead` alternated 51 ↔ 75 m and the target −0.34 ↔ +0.65 every few steps). The steering followed (±0.45), so `allowed` alternated 167 ↔ 242 km/h with brake/throttle chatter: 150 km/h at ~1,240 m where v0.31 did 187. Steering reversals 71 → **165**; mean sideways speed 1.27 → 1.60 m/s. Section losses: ~600 m +0.23 s, ~1,200 m +0.22 s, ~400 m +0.18 s, ~1,900 and ~2,400 m +0.10 s; gains only ~3,100–3,300 m (−0.18 s). Hairpin: 71.5 km/h minimum (v0.31 66.3) and exit −0.58 (v0.31 −0.59). Flick sideways speed 14.0 → 15.3 km/h. Slowest corner 72 km/h (~3,275 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ❌ Rejected — 1.08 s slower, with heavy steering oscillation, and the car still did not reach the outside line. Driver code and tuning card reverted to v0.31 (`git checkout v0.31 -- snakeoil3_v1.py tuning-card.md`). |
| **Learned** | **The line target must not depend on the car's own nose.** The target was switched by `\|aim\|` > 2° and set by `ahead` (nose beams); a stronger line pull swings the nose, which moves both, which moves the target: a feedback loop. v0.31 has the same loop in a weaker form (81 target jumps per lap). Before the pull can be strengthened, the target has to be steady: hold the corner state (hysteresis on `aim`) and take the corner progress from a yaw-free measure. In replay on both logs the road length along the track direction (`beam_at(−angle)`) changes 0.86 m per step in corners against 1.4–1.6 m for the nose, and gives the same inside windows. Capping the line's steering at 0.21 also made the car too slow to move across: ~0.23 `trackPos`/s at ~1.5°. |

## v0.33 — Steady racing-line target: bend held until the bearing falls below `line_aim_off` 1°, phase from the road along the track direction

| Field | Detail |

The racing-line target (lines 582–599) no longer follows the car's own nose: (1) a bend starts when the look-ahead bearing `\|aim\|` passes 2° (side = its sign, as before) but now lasts until `\|aim\|` falls below the new `line_aim_off=1` (line 544); in between, the side is held between steps (`c.line_side`); (2) the bend's progress (outside ≥ 80 m, inside ≤ 40 m) is measured as the road visible **along the track direction** (`beam_at(−angle ± 0.5°)`, longest of three) instead of `ahead`, which uses the nose beams. `line_offset` 0.5, `line_gain` 0.50 and the phase thresholds are unchanged; `ahead` for the braking plan is unchanged. |
| **Why** | v0.32 showed the line target is a feedback loop: the line's steering swings the nose, the nose beams change `ahead` (51 ↔ 75 m at ~1,190–1,260 m) and `aim` (crossing 2° 142 times a lap), and both set the target, which jumped by more than 0.2 in 261 steps; the user saw the wheel oscillate a lot. v0.31 has the same loop in a weaker form: 81 target jumps and 64 crossings of 2° per lap. A stronger line pull (the user's priority, plan C) cannot work until the target is steady. In corners the road along the track direction changes 0.86 m per step on both logs against 1.4–1.6 m for the nose `ahead`. Offline on the logged signals, each part alone removes only part of the jumps (v0.31 log: hysteresis 81 → 70, track-direction phase 81 → 59; both 81 → 44; v0.32 log 148 → 44), so the two go together as one mechanism. |

Target jumps 81 → 44; steering reversals 75 → 61 (the replayed v0.31 gives 75 against 71 logged). Fewer reversals at ~3,500 m (8 → 1), ~2,300 m (4 → 2), ~2,500 m (8 → 6). The inside windows stay within a few metres of v0.31's in every corner (e.g. ~2,935–3,011 m, hairpin ~3,235–3,286 m), so the apexes do not move; the hairpin entry is identical on the v0.31 log. One change of note: at the flick (~2,471–2,473 m) the bend is held while `aim` falls from 2° to 1.2°, so the left-bend pull stays on ~3 steps longer and the wheel stays at full lock ~60 ms longer. Driven: steering calmer in corners (logged reversals below 71), lap time about the same as v0.31 (1:31.37, ±0.2 s), `\|trackPos\|` about as in v0.31 (max ~0.6). If it is a little slower but calmer it would be kept as an enabling change for a stronger line pull. Watch the flick's sideways speed (14.0 km/h in v0.31) and the hairpin exit (−0.59). Reject on damage or leaving the track. Damage expected to stay at 0. |

| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 213 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 66 km/h |
| **Observed** | Car completed a full lap with zero damage and the damage stop never triggered. Lap time 0.34 s faster than v0.31 (1:31.37 → 1:31.03) — **the fastest lap so far** (v0.28 1:31.24), better than the predicted ±0.2 s. Top speed 213 km/h. Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_193205.csv`): **steering calmer as intended**: reversals 71 → **38**, target jumps 81 → 38, mean sideways speed 1.27 → 1.29 m/s. Gains spread over many sections (~100 m −0.07 s, ~1,100 m −0.05, ~1,600 m −0.06, ~2,000 m −0.06, ~2,300 m −0.06; loss ~2,700 m +0.05). Flick sideways speed 14.0 → 13.3 km/h (~2,400 m) and 7.6 → 6.9 (~2,500 m); but ~2,384 m (flick approach, 152 km/h) showed 14.3 km/h sideways (v0.31 7.9 in that section). Hairpin: 66 km/h, exit −0.59 → **−0.65** (max `\|trackPos\|` of the lap, ~3,288 m). Mean `\|trackPos\|` 0.13 → 0.17. **The user saw the car go out on exits but not use the full radius.** Measured per corner (+ = outside): entries −0.18 to +0.09, apexes only −0.01 to −0.24 on the inside, exits reach only +0.14 to +0.27 (~979 m +0.27, ~1,473 m +0.14, ~2,937 m +0.20; ~2,654 m only −0.07) — the car uses about ±0.25 of the ±1 available outside the flick and the hairpin. On the exits the line's pull outward (+0.37 → +0.12) is cancelled by the heading term `angle·15/π` (−0.21 → −0.01), so the net steering is ≈ 0 and the car drifts out only ~0.5 in ~100 m. Slowest corner 66 km/h (~3,286 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — fastest lap so far (−0.34 s), steering reversals halved |
| **Learned** | A steady target pays on its own: removing the target's feedback through the nose beams halved the steering reversals and gained 0.34 s with the same line. The line itself is still narrow (±0.25 of ±1): any line pull is balanced by the heading term at a car angle of ~2–3°, and moving the car across the full width in a corner's length needs larger angles, which the braking look-ahead (`ahead`, track direction only within 3°) does not tolerate (v0.25 false braking). Maximising the radius therefore needs a look-ahead / braking plan that stays correct when the car is angled more than 3°. |

## v0.34 — Corner speed from sharpness: `turn_grip` 6 → 7

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.34 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.34`). `turn_grip` 6.0 → 7.0 m/s² (line 550): the sideways acceleration the sharpness plan assumes when the car curves onto a beam, `v = √(turn_grip·radius)`. Grip-limited corner speeds rise by √(7/6) ≈ +8%. Step 2 of the merged improvement plan (`improvement-plan.md`). |
| **Why** | The sharpness plan was the biggest gain so far (v0.27, −6.78 s), and 6 m/s² was a first step: the medium corners run at ≤ 8.4 km/h sideways and `\|steer\|` ≤ 0.54 (v0.31), and the hairpin shows ≥ 14 m/s² of grip. Half the medium corners were held by this grip value in v0.27. The user's plan raises it in steps of 1 (7 → 8 → 9) and stops at the first sign of trouble. |
| **Prediction** | Replay with the real `drive_example()` of v0.33 and v0.34 on every logged v0.33 row (open loop): steering identical; the allowed speed rises in 793 of 4,322 steps (up to +15.5 km/h where a corner is grip-limited), brake differs in 287 steps, throttle in 487. Braking steps fall in ~400 m (67 → 31), ~500 m (9 → 0), ~700 m (63 → 49), ~1,500 m (16 → 4), ~2,400 m (100 → 81) and ~2,900 m (69 → 60). At the flick (~2,418–2,450 m) and the hairpin (~3,231–3,251 m) the plan is off at full lock, but the approach braking eases ~0.1 pedal, so each enters ~2 km/h faster. Driven: medium-corner minimums a few km/h higher (e.g. ~485, ~1,523, ~1,926, ~2,983 m), sideways speed there up from ≤ 8.4 toward ~9–10 km/h, lap roughly 0.3–1.0 s faster than 1:31.03. Risks: the hairpin (v0.33 66 km/h, exit −0.65; 73 km/h gave −0.86 and 75 km/h left the track) and the flick (13.3 km/h sideways; its approach at ~2,384 m already 14.3). Reject on damage or leaving the track, or if medium-corner sideways speed climbs toward ~14 km/h. Damage expected to stay at 0. |
| **Lap time** | 1:30.74 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 213 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 65 km/h |
| **Observed** | Car completed a full lap with zero damage and the damage stop never triggered. Lap time 0.29 s faster than v0.33 (1:31.03 → 1:30.74) — **the fastest lap so far**, at the low end of the predicted 0.3–1.0 s. Top speed 213 km/h. Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_194740.csv`): medium-corner minimums up where the plan was grip-limited — ~100 m 135 → 141 km/h, ~200 m 137 → 142, ~400 m 86 → 89, ~1,900 m 91 → 94; section gains ~400 m −0.06 s, ~2,800 m −0.05, ~500/600 m −0.03 each; nothing lost by more than 0.03 s. Sideways speed in the medium corners at most 9.1 km/h (~1,900 m; v0.33 8.3), far from the ~14 km/h reject level. **Hairpin and flick safer, not worse:** hairpin 65 km/h with exit −0.65 → **−0.56**; flick sideways speed 13.3 → **9.5** km/h (~2,400 m), 6.9 → 6.3 (~2,500 m). Max `\|trackPos\|` 0.56 (~3,288 m). Steering reversals 39. **What limits the plan now (classified offline for every braking step):** of 1,299 braking steps, 1,248 are held by braking distance (a beam's length with `brake_decel` 11 down to `corner_speed`, less `brake_margin`), only 15 by grip (`turn_grip`), 34 at full lock (plan off), 2 by the road ahead. While braking the car runs a median 3.2 km/h over the allowed speed (p90 4.8) with a median pedal of 0.16; measured deceleration: pedal 0.1–0.2 → 11.1 m/s², 0.2–0.3 → 12.5, 0.3–0.5 → 17.8. Slowest corner 65 km/h (~3,284 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — fastest lap so far (−0.29 s), flick and hairpin calmer |
| **Learned** | At `turn_grip` 7 the sharpness plan is almost never grip-limited any more: 1,248 of 1,299 braking steps are held by braking distance. A replay of `turn_grip` 8 on this lap changed the brake in only 34 steps (7 gave 287), so plan step 2 stops at 7: the lever is now braking (`brake_gain`, `brake_decel`, `brake_margin`), plan step 3. |

## v0.35 — Brake pedal follows the plan more closely: `brake_gain` 0.05 → 0.08

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.35 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.35`). `brake_gain` 0.05 → 0.08 (line 540): brake pedal per km/h over the allowed speed, so full brake now comes at 12.5 km/h over (was 20). Step 3a of `improvement-plan.md`. |
| **Why** | v0.34: 1,248 of 1,299 braking steps are held by braking distance, so braking is now the lever. With gain 0.05 the car must run a median 3.2 km/h (p90 4.8) over the allowed speed to press the pedal to its median 0.16, i.e. it runs past the planned braking curve and then brakes in on/off phases (81 per lap). The car can do more: pedal 0.2–0.3 gave 12.5 m/s², 0.3–0.5 gave 17.8 m/s² (16.4 m/s² at pedal 0.57 in v0.31). A higher gain makes the pedal track the curve with less over-speed, which is needed before the plan's deceleration (`brake_decel`) is raised in step 3b. `turn_grip` 8 was skipped (see v0.34 Learned). |
| **Prediction** | Replay with the real `drive_example()` of v0.34 and v0.35 on every logged v0.34 row (open loop): only the brake pedal differs (1,301 steps); steering and throttle identical. At the logged over-speeds the pedal would be median 0.16 → 0.26, p90 0.24 → 0.38, ≥ 0.5 in 10 → 33 steps, full brake in 1 → 7. Driven (closed loop) the car should instead ride closer to the allowed curve: over-speed while braking ~3.2 → ~2 km/h, pedal about the same as before or a little higher, braking phases a little smoother. Because it follows the planned curve more tightly, it brakes slightly earlier on average, so the lap is roughly the same (±0.15 s, possibly a little slower); it is an enabling change for raising `brake_decel` (12.5) next. Watch: ABS (wheel lock), sideways speed on corner entry, the flick and the hairpin entries. Reject on damage or leaving the track. Damage expected to stay at 0. |
| **Lap time** | 1:31.18 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 213 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 66 km/h |
| **Observed** | Car completed a full lap with zero damage and the damage stop never triggered, but it was **0.44 s slower** than v0.34 (1:30.74 → 1:31.18), outside the predicted ±0.15 s. Top speed 213 km/h. Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_195941.csv`): the pedal followed the plan as intended — over-speed while braking median 3.2 → **2.2** km/h (p90 4.8 → 3.1), pedal median 0.16 → 0.18, braking time 27.3 → 26.3 s — but braking phases rose 81 → 91, and the loss is spread evenly: ~+0.02 s in almost every 100 m section with braking. Hairpin 66 km/h, exit −0.60; flick sideways speed 9.5 → 8.4 km/h; max `\|trackPos\|` 0.61. **Very hard braking at high speed:** where the pedal reached 0.5–1.0 at ~180–200 km/h (~1,235, ~2,058, ~3,588 m) the car slowed ~2.3 km/h per step, about **30 m/s²**; at pedal 0.3–0.5, 18–21 m/s² (very likely aerodynamic downforce). **Corkscrew (user: the car jumps off the ground in the transition):** at ~2,475–2,490 m the rear wheels spin up to +19 (rearSpin) with almost no throttle, then −22 under 0.19 pedal, sideways speed swings −8 → +8 km/h and rpm drops to ~7,000 — the wheels unload or leave the ground, so wheel-speed readings (traction control, ABS, slip and deceleration estimates) are not reliable there. Slowest corner 66 km/h (~3,285 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ❌ Rejected — 0.44 s slower. Driver code and tuning card reverted to v0.34 (`git checkout v0.34 -- snakeoil3_v1.py tuning-card.md`). |
| **Learned** | With `brake_gain` 0.05 the ~3 km/h the car runs over the allowed speed is not a defect: it effectively brakes a little later than the plan, on a curve that the car can follow. A tighter pedal just brakes earlier and costs time. The lever is the plan itself (`brake_decel`), so step 3b goes ahead on `brake_gain` 0.05. The car can decelerate far harder than 11 m/s² at high speed (~30 m/s² at ~190 km/h with full pedal), so a speed-dependent `brake_decel` is a strong follow-up. Telemetry in the Corkscrew transition (~2,475–2,490 m) includes airborne/unloaded wheels and must be excluded from slip and deceleration measurements. |

With `brake_gain` 0.05 the car runs ~3 km/h over the curve, and pedal 0.25 (5 km/h over) gives ~12.5 m/s², so 12.5 is a step the pedal can still deliver. v0.35 showed that tightening the pedal alone costs time. |

That is the watch point for higher values and an argument for trail-braking logic (pedal tapered as `\|steer\|` rises) later in step 3. |

## v0.38 — Ease the brake when the car slides: `slide_brake` 5 km/h

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.38 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.38`). New knob `slide_brake=5` (line 556) and a new step after the brake is set (lines 643–649): while braking, if the sideways speed `\|speedY\|` is over `slide_brake` km/h, the brake is halved (before ABS, which may halve it again). Part of plan step 3 (braking while turning / trail braking). |
| **Why** | v0.36–v0.37 made the slides where the car brakes while turning grow with every `brake_decel` step: the kink at ~2,385 m 14.3 → 16.4 → 18.3 km/h, the flick to 19.4 km/h with `\|trackPos\|` 0.70. Braking puts grip on the front tyres and the rear steps out. A limit from `\|steer\|` alone was tried in replay and rejected before running: it hardly touched the kink (2 steps; there the slide grows at `\|steer\|` 0.1–0.17 at ~170 km/h) and mostly removed braking at the hairpin entry (14 steps, ~12 km/h less slowing — dangerous). Reacting to the slide itself targets exactly the slides: straight braking stays under ~5 km/h sideways (57–65 of ~1,000–1,160 braking steps per lap are above it), and our data shows a pedal under 0.1 lets the sideways speed settle while 0.1–0.3 grows it. Same idea as ABS (halve when a wheel locks), applied to the rear stepping out. |
| **Prediction** | Replay with the real `drive_example()` of v0.37 and v0.38 on every logged v0.37 row (open loop): only the brake differs, in 64 steps — the kink ~2,300 m (18 steps), the flick entry ~2,400 m (22), ~400 m (16, tiny), ~1,900 m (6), one at the hairpin (~3,200 m) and one at ~1,500 m; steering, throttle and gears identical. Driven: kink sideways speed 18.3 → roughly 10–13 km/h; the flick entry slide smaller (~8 instead of 10.8 km/h) — but the flick's left → right switch (~2,476 m) happens off the brake, so that part may change little. The car slows a little less while sliding (a few km/h, caught by straight braking after the kink), so the lap is about the same (±0.15 s). It is a safety/enabling change before braking is pushed further (speed-dependent `brake_decel`, `brake_margin`). Reject on damage or leaving the track. Damage expected to stay at 0. |
| **Lap time** | 1:27.57 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 212 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 65 km/h |
| **Observed** | Car completed a full lap with zero damage and stayed on track. Lap time 0.02 s faster than v0.37 (1:27.59 → 1:27.57; confirmed by the user against the screen: 1:27:57, 212 km/h, 0 damage). Telemetry (`runs/run_20261001_203129.csv`): **the slide brake barely acted on its target**: kink (~2,385 m) 18.3 → 17.4 km/h sideways, flick entry 11.0 → 10.9, braking steps with `\|speedY\|` > 5 km/h unchanged at 65 — by the time the slide passes 5 km/h it is established, and half the pedal (0.1) still lets it grow. **The flick's left → right switch got worse**: 19.4 → **25.7** km/h sideways, max `\|trackPos\|` 0.72 (~2,478 m). That slide happens off the brake: after the switch (~2,484–2,499 m) the stored throttle climbs to 1.0 while the rear wheels spin (the car has just come off the Corkscrew crest), and traction control cuts the sent throttle to 0 and releases it to ~1.0 the next step, alternating for ~20 steps; each release kicks the rear loose again. **First automatic run:** `run_race.py` started TORCS from the race file (`wtorcs.exe -r`), and the run (`runs/run_20261001_203505.csv`) reproduced this manual run in all 4,220 rows; it also logs past the line, where `lastLapTime` gives TORCS's own time (87.57). Slowest corner 65 km/h (~3,284 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ❌ Rejected — no effect on the slides it targeted, and the flick slide grew (25.7 km/h). Driver code and tuning card reverted to v0.37 (`git checkout v0.37 -- snakeoil3_v1.py tuning-card.md`). |
| **Learned** | A brake ease triggered by the sideways speed comes too late: once the car slides at 5 km/h, half the pedal still grows the slide. The flick's biggest slide is not braking at all but traction-control chatter on the Corkscrew exit (throttle sent 0 ↔ 1 under sustained rear spin), plan step 4b. With automatic runs (`run_race.py`, ~4 s per lap) candidate values can now be tried closed-loop instead of only replayed open-loop. |

## v0.39 — Traction-control cut fades out instead of releasing at once: `tc_hold` 0.8

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.39 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.39`), built on v0.37 (v0.38 rejected). New knob `tc_hold=.8` (line 554). Traction control (lines 659–675): the cut is now `max(new cut, tc_hold × last step's cut)`, kept in `c.tc_cut`, so after a burst of wheelspin it fades over a few steps (0.8 per step, ~10 steps to 10%) instead of vanishing the next step. Only the throttle sent is cut, as before; the stored throttle is untouched. Telemetry only (no driving effect): `curLapTime` and `lastLapTime` are logged with 3 decimals (line 726). Plan step 4b, taken early as a safety fix for the flick. |
| **Why** | v0.38 showed the flick's worst slide (25.7 km/h, `\|trackPos\|` 0.72) is traction-control chatter on the Corkscrew exit: the cut releases at once, the throttle snaps back to ~1.0, the unloaded rear spins again, and the sent throttle alternates 0 ↔ 1 for ~20 steps. Across the lap the sent throttle jumped by more than 0.5 in 363 steps (v0.37). A cut that fades out gives a steady partial throttle under sustained spin. The value was chosen from closed-loop trials (`run_race.py`, full laps): `tc_hold` 0 (= v0.37) 87.596 s, flick switch 19.4 km/h, max `\|trackPos\|` 0.696, 363 throttle jumps; 0.5 87.55 / 16.5 / 0.678 / 66; 0.7 87.50 / 15.7 / 0.665 / 47; 0.75 87.43 / 13.7 / 0.643 / 40; **0.8 87.45 / 11.7 / 0.625 / 40**; 0.85 87.48 / 14.9 / 0.643 / 37; 0.9 87.51 / 9.8 / 0.592 / 33. 0.8 is among the fastest with the most flick margin of those; differences of ~0.03 s between neighbours are within the run-to-run sensitivity of small changes. |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (closed-loop trial, see Why) rather than predicted: about 1:27.44–1:27.45, flick calmer. Reject on damage or leaving the track. |
| **Lap time** | 1:27.44 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 213 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 64 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically with `run_race.py` (`runs/run_20261001_203931.csv`); TORCS time **87.448 s** → on screen 1:27:44 (the screen truncates: v0.37 was 87.596 s → 1:27:59). Lap 0.15 s faster than v0.37 (1:27.59 → 1:27.44) — **the fastest lap so far**. Zero damage, on track. **Flick:** left → right switch 19.4 → **11.7** km/h sideways, max `\|trackPos\|` 0.696 → **0.625** (~2,479 m). **Throttle:** sent-throttle jumps > 0.5 363 → **40**; traction control now cuts for 18.6 s of the lap (v0.37 10.8 s) but gently, full throttle 28% → 16%, mean sideways speed 1.70 → 1.60 m/s. Gains mostly on corner exits (~800 m −0.06 s, hairpin exit ~3,300 m −0.05 s). Kink (~2,385 m) unchanged at 18.3 km/h (a braking slide; v0.38 did not fix it). Hairpin exit −0.53. Slowest corner 64 km/h (~3,282 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — fastest lap so far (−0.15 s), flick slide 19.4 → 11.7 km/h, throttle chatter gone |
| **Learned** | Releasing the traction-control cut at once (v0.28) made the throttle chatter under sustained spin; fading it is both safer and faster, even though full throttle drops from 28% to 16% of the lap — the throttle that was being sent only spun the wheels. The time on screen is TORCS's time truncated to hundredths; logs now carry 3 decimals. Closed-loop trials with `run_race.py` replace open-loop replays for picking values; neighbouring values differ by a few hundredths in a non-monotonic way, so prefer values with margin over the single fastest. |

## v0.40 — Braking plan expects more deceleration at speed: `brake_aero` 0.004

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.40 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.40`). New knob `brake_aero=.004` (line 539). The braking plan (lines 606–619) now assumes a deceleration of `brake_decel + brake_aero·v²` (v in m/s) instead of a flat `brake_decel`, for both the road ahead and every beam of the sharpness plan (`brake_speed(d)`, used at line 636). Slowing from v to the corner speed v0 over d metres then gives v² = ((a + c·v0²)·e^(2c·d) − a)/c, which is v0² + 2ad when c = 0. At 100 km/h the plan assumes 14 + 3.1 = 17.1 m/s², at 150 km/h 14 + 6.9 = 20.9, at 200 km/h 14 + 12.3 = 26.3. Plan step 3c. |
| **Why** | Braking distance still limited ~95% of braking steps (v0.37), and the car slows harder at speed. Measured on straight braking (pedal 0.15–0.4, `\|steer\|` < 0.15, v0.36–v0.39, Corkscrew transition excluded): deceleration per unit of pedal 50–55 m/s² from 80 to 160 km/h, 60 at 160–180, 63 at 180–200, 72 at 200–220 (drag and downforce); v0.35 showed ~30 m/s² at full pedal at ~190 km/h. A flat 14 m/s² leaves that unused exactly where braking zones are longest. |
| **Prediction** | Chosen from closed-loop trials (full laps with `run_race.py`): `brake_aero` 0 → 87.45 s (= v0.39); 0.001 → 86.82; 0.002 → 86.59; 0.003 → 86.25 (max `\|trackPos\|` 0.733); **0.004 → 85.95** (0.706); 0.005 → 85.42 (0.707); 0.006 → 85.03 (0.722); **0.007 → left the track** (`\|trackPos\|` 1.064); **0.008 → left the track at the flick** (−1.94 at ~2,467 m, run ended). All finished laps had 0 damage. 0.004 is ~40% below the first failure; 0.005–0.006 are faster but next to it. Reject on damage or leaving the track. |
| **Lap time** | 1:25.95 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 214 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 64 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261001_204628.csv`); TORCS time **85.954 s** → on screen 1:25:95. Lap **1.49 s faster** than v0.39 (1:27.44 → 1:25.95) — **the fastest lap so far**. Zero damage, on track (max `\|trackPos\|` 0.706 at ~2,477 m, the flick). Gains in every high-speed braking zone: ~300 m −0.16 s, ~1,800 m −0.14, ~900 m −0.13, ~1,400 m −0.12, ~600 m −0.11, ~2,900 m −0.11, ~2,300 m −0.10, ~2,600 m −0.09, ~3,100/3,200 m −0.09 each; entry speeds into the braking zones up (e.g. ~2,900 m max 176 → 207 km/h, ~3,100 m min 166 → 193). Braking 1,039 (v0.37) → 779 steps (16.4 s); the car runs a median 7.5 km/h over the allowed speed (p90 10.6) with a median pedal of 0.36 (p90 0.53). **The kink (~2,385 m) slide fell 18.3 → 10.5 km/h**: braking now ends before the kink's turn instead of running through it. Flick switch 11.7 → 17.3 km/h. Hairpin 64 km/h, exit −0.60. Top speed 214 km/h. Slowest corner 64 km/h (~3,283 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — fastest lap so far (−1.49 s) |
| **Learned** | The braking plan was the limit, and the car's real deceleration rises with speed: a speed-dependent plan gained 1.49 s at a conservative setting and also cured the kink slide (braking finishes before the turn). The new limit is the flick: from `brake_aero` 0.007 the car arrives too fast and leaves the track there. The pedal (gain 0.05) now runs ~7.5 km/h over the plan to reach the higher deceleration, so `brake_gain` may be worth a new look at these levels. |

## v0.41 — Straights no longer capped at ~210 km/h: `target_speed` 200 → 250

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.41 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.41`). `target_speed` 200 → 250 km/h (line 536, comment added; no other line moved). The throttle now aims for 250 km/h on straights instead of 200; corners are still set by the braking plan (`allowed_speed`). Not a step of `improvement-plan.md`: an own candidate from the v0.40 telemetry. |
| **Why** | **Inherited conclusion challenged:** "straights are close to used up" (v0.17: 180 → 200 gained only 0.18 s). That was measured when the plan braked at 8–11 m/s². Since v0.36–v0.40 the plan brakes at 14 + 0.004·v² m/s² (26 at 200 km/h), so braking zones from high speed are short, yet in v0.40 the throttle eased off before every long braking zone: e.g. ~300–348 m it fell 0.90 → 0.57 at 204–212 km/h while still accelerating, and 1,200–1,300 m, 1,700 m, 2,100–2,200 m and 3,100 m were driven at a flat 200–211 km/h with no full throttle. 5th gear was never left (0 steps in 6th). **Candidates, all closed-loop full laps with `run_race.py` on v0.40 (TORCS s):** baseline v0.40 reproduced at 85.954. (1) **`target_speed`** 220 → 84.906; 230 → 84.560; 240 → 84.384; **250 → 84.358**; 260, 270 and 300 → 84.416 (identical: top 255.6, the cap no longer binds). Flick `\|trackPos\|` 0.71–0.73, kink 10.5–11.1 km/h, hairpin 64 km/h in all of them: no failure point on this axis. (2) **`turn_grip`** (second challenge: v0.34 skipped 8/9 as "not grip-limited", counted over braking steps only; but in medium corners the car rides the allowed speed on part throttle, e.g. ~410–510 m at 96–100 km/h where the sharpness plan sets `allowed` ~98 against ~90 from the road ahead, so grip does bind there): 8 → 85.130 (flick `\|trackPos\|` 0.797, kink 13.0 km/h, hairpin exit 0.69); 9 → 84.838 (0.752, 11.9, 0.70). Real gains (−0.82/−1.12 s), but smaller than (1) and they eat flick and hairpin margin. (3) **`corner_speed`** 75 → 80 → 89.422, flick slide 46.8 km/h, `\|trackPos\|` 1.024 at ~2,503 m (off track). (4) **`brake_margin`** 15 → 10 → left the track at the flick (−1.30 at ~2,483 m, run ended at 2,492 m). (5) **`brake_gain`** 0.05 → 0.08 (re-test of v0.35 at the new deceleration levels) → 86.250, +0.30 s: still only brakes earlier. `target_speed` won by the most time while leaving every watch point unchanged; 250 is the fastest measured value and nothing fails above it. For the next agent (informational, two knobs, not this version): 250 + `turn_grip` 9 → 83.166 (flick `\|trackPos\|` 0.808, kink 15.4 km/h); 250 + `brake_aero` 0.005 → 83.686 (flick 0.655). |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (closed-loop trial, see Why): 84.358 s. Risks: braking zones now start from up to ~250 km/h, so watch the pedal (full pedal possible), sideways speed under braking, the kink (~2,385 m), the flick and the hairpin. Reject on damage or leaving the track. |
| **Lap time** | 1:24.35 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 254 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 64 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261001_205914.csv`); TORCS time **84.358 s** → on screen 1:24:35. Lap **1.60 s faster** than v0.40 (1:25.95 → 1:24.35) — **the fastest lap so far**, ~0.35 s from the ~1:24 target. Zero damage, on track (max `\|trackPos\|` 0.713 at ~2,477 m, the flick; v0.40 0.706). Gains on every long straight: ~2,200 m −0.28 s, ~1,300 m −0.26, ~3,500 m −0.22, ~1,800 m −0.16, ~1,200 m −0.14, ~1,400 m −0.12, ~2,100 m −0.12, ~1,700 m −0.10, ~2,300 m −0.08, ~3,100 m −0.04; small losses ~1,900 m +0.02, ~2,600/2,700 m +0.01 each. Top speed 214 → 254 km/h (~1,400 m); 6th gear now used (505 steps); sent throttle > 0.95 in 39% of the lap (v0.40 23%). Braking 779 → 856 steps, over-speed median 7.7 km/h (p90 11.5), pedal median 0.38 (p90 0.57); **full pedal for the first time**, 9 steps at ~2,323–2,335 m (229 → 202 km/h, up to 23 km/h over the plan, straight, sideways ≤ 0.5 km/h, ABS not triggered). Kink 10.5 → 10.7 km/h sideways, flick switch 17.3 → 17.2, hairpin 64 km/h, exit 0.60 → 0.59. Slowest corner 64 km/h (~3,283 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — fastest lap so far (−1.60 s) |
| **Learned** | The 200 km/h cap was the biggest single limit left: "straights used up" was true only while braking was weak. With the speed-dependent plan, raising the cap costs nothing in the braking zones (each corner's entry speed is still set by `allowed_speed`) and the watch points did not move. The car tops out at ~255 km/h on the longest straight, so caps above ~255 change nothing. The ~2,300 m braking zone now needs full pedal (23 km/h over the plan): the pedal gain 0.05 is near its end there, so a brake plan that brakes harder still will need the pedal to keep up. `turn_grip` is grip-limiting again in medium corners (9 → −1.12 s on v0.40), contrary to the v0.34 count. |

## v0.42 — Shift up just under the limiter: `upshift_rpm` 15,000 → 18,500

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.42 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.42`). `upshift_rpm` 15,000 → 18,500 (line 547, comment updated; no other line moved). The car now shifts up just under the rev limiter (18,700) instead of 2,000 rpm below the end of the torque plateau. Downshifts (`downshift_rpm` 13,500, `lowest_running_gear` 2) are unchanged. Plan step 6 (gearbox), taken early and further than the plan's ~17,000 because the trials said so. |
| **Why** | **Own analysis of v0.41 first:** time by limiter: full throttle 33.2 s, traction-control cut 20.1 s, braking 17.8 s, riding the allowed speed 7.1 s, part throttle 3.8 s, full lock 2.1 s. The car spends most of the lap accelerating, and at 15,000 rpm every upshift landed at ~12,000–13,500 rpm (ratio steps 1.09–1.26). From `car1-ow1.xml`, power still rises to 679 kW at 18,000 rpm (360 N·m) and is ~664 kW at the limiter, against 558 kW at 15,000 and ~470 kW at 13,000: with these close ratios the lower gear near the limiter always gives more power than the next gear. v0.41 also made 66 shifts with 6 quick reversals. **Inherited conclusion challenged:** v0.6 ("18,000/17,000 rpm shifting slower, likely wheelspin") and the plan's "~17,000, 0.3–1 s". v0.6 had no traction control and the speed-based box; with the v0.28–v0.39 traction control the late shift costs nothing at any watch point. Also re-checked plan step 4a (brake dead band, "light brake touches reset the throttle"): no gain now (see below). **Candidates, closed-loop full laps with `run_race.py` on v0.41 (TORCS s; baseline reproduced at 84.358):** (1) **`upshift_rpm`** 16,000 → 83.888; 17,000 → 83.628; 17,500 → 83.468; 17,800 → 83.308; 18,000 → 83.348; 18,200 → 83.260; 18,400 → 83.252; **18,500 → 83.128**; 18,600 → 83.106; 18,700 → 83.170; 19,000 → 83.008; 19,500 → 82.954; **20,000 → 91.594 (never leaves 2nd at speed, top 170 km/h)**; 21,000 → 109.568. Flick `\|trackPos\|` 0.710–0.730, kink 10.2–11.0 km/h, hairpin 64 km/h in every finished trial: the change does not touch the corners. 19,000–19,500 shift only because the logged rpm overshoots the limiter (up to 19,464), so they rely on that overshoot; 18,500 triggers on a real reading 200 rpm under the limiter. (2) **`turn_grip`** 8 → 83.506 (flick 0.785); 9 → 83.166 (flick 0.808, kink 15.4 km/h, hairpin 0.695); 10 → 83.012 (flick 0.875, flick slide 23.4 km/h): real gains, but each step eats flick margin. (3) **`brake_aero`** 0.005 → 83.686 (flick 0.655); 0.006 → 83.540 (0.720). (4) **`tc_slip`** 3.0 → 84.144 (flick slide 20.1 km/h); 2.0 → 85.050. (5) **`downshift_rpm`** 14,500 → 98.160, 15,500 → 108.782, 16,500 → 108.086 (gear hunting against the 15,000 upshift). (6) **Brake dead band** (brake only when 1/2/3 km/h over the allowed speed, plan 4a) → 84.520 / 84.330 / 84.356: no gain. (7) **New mechanism, full-lock brake** (above `\|steer\|` 0.95 and 50 km/h: pedal at least 0.05, throttle 0; braking at full lock loads the front tyres, which are saturated at the flick: the car runs from `trackPos` +0.1 to −0.7 at full lock and the 75 km/h floor) → 84.450: flick `\|trackPos\|` 0.713 → **0.529**, flick slide 17.2 → 12.1 km/h, hairpin `\|trackPos\|` 0.594 → **0.335**, but hairpin 64 → 52 km/h; pedal 0.15 → 84.706 (flick 0.354, hairpin 49 km/h). `upshift_rpm` won: the most time of any single change with margin left, and no watch point moved. **For the next agent (informational, two or three knobs on 18,500):** + `turn_grip` 9 → 81.802 (flick 0.837, kink 17.5 km/h); + `brake_aero` 0.006 → 82.088 (flick 0.754); + full-lock brake 0.05 → 83.182 (flick 0.532); + full-lock brake 0.05 + `corner_speed` 80 → 82.466 (flick 0.621; 80 alone left the track on v0.40); + full-lock brake 0.05 + `turn_grip` 10 → 81.768 (flick 0.566, kink 17.8 km/h, hairpin 0.428); + `brake_aero` 0.008, with or without the full-lock brake → **left the track** at ~2,467 m (2.015 / 1.787). |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (closed-loop trial, see Why): 83.128 s. Risks: more wheelspin in low gears (traction control should absorb it), the limiter in 2nd/3rd exits. Reject on damage or leaving the track. |
| **Lap time** | 1:23.12 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 256 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 64 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261001_211338.csv`); TORCS time **83.128 s** → on screen 1:23:12. Lap **1.23 s faster** than v0.41 (1:24.35 → 1:23.12) — **the fastest lap so far**, and below the ~1:24 target. Zero damage, on track (max `\|trackPos\|` 0.718 at ~2,477 m, the flick; v0.41 0.713). Gains wherever the car accelerates: ~1,700 m −0.10 s, ~1,100 m −0.09, ~2,800 m −0.09, ~3,400 m −0.09, ~1,300 m −0.08, ~1,600/2,000/2,100/2,200 m −0.08 each, ~100/800/3,000/3,100/3,500 m −0.07 each; small losses ~700 m +0.02, ~2,300 m +0.02, ~2,900 m +0.02, ~500 m +0.01. Upshifts at 18,500–18,955 rpm (2→3 at ~151 km/h, 3→4 ~194, 4→5 ~243); shifts 66 → 41, quick gear reversals (< 60 m) 6 → 0; rpm above 18,400 in 46 steps. **6th gear is no longer used**: the top speed of 256.8 km/h (~1,394 m) is reached in 5th (limiter ~294 km/h). Braking steps 856 → 890. Flick slide 17.2 → 17.9 km/h, kink 10.7 → 10.4, hairpin 64 km/h, exit −0.60. Slowest corner 64 km/h (~3,283 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — fastest lap so far (−1.23 s), corners untouched |
| **Learned** | Shifting at 15,000 rpm threw away power on every exit: with ratio steps of only 9–26%, the engine (power rising to 18,000 rpm, ~664 kW at the limiter) is best held to the limiter. v0.6's "late shifting is slower" belonged to a car without traction control and does not hold now. The gain is all on acceleration, so it stacks with the corner knobs (18,500 + `turn_grip` 9 ran 81.80). The gearbox hunting the plan wanted to fix separately is gone as a side effect (reversals 6 → 0). Plan 4a (brake dead band) gave nothing at 1–3 km/h. The flick is still the shared failure point; a full-lock brake (new, tested) cut its `\|trackPos\|` from 0.71 to 0.53 and made `corner_speed` 80 finish, so it is a candidate enabling change. |

## v0.43 — Traction control lets the rear spin more on straights: `tc_slip_straight` 5.0

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.43 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.43`). Three new knobs for one mechanism (lines 556–558): `tc_slip_straight=5.0` (m/s), `tc_slip_steer=.7`, `tc_slip_slide=8` (km/h). The traction-control limit (line 695) is now `tc_slip + tc_slip_straight·clip(1 − \|steer\|/tc_slip_steer, 0, 1)·clip(1 − \|speedY\|/tc_slip_slide, 0, 1)`: 7.5 m/s of rear over-speed with the wheel straight and the car not sliding, falling linearly to the old 2.5 m/s at `\|steer\|` 0.7 or at 8 km/h sideways. Everything else (`tc_gain` 0.5, `tc_hold` 0.8, the full-lock throttle limit) is unchanged. New comment block in the traction-control section (lines 685–692); everything below moved down by 3 (knobs) and 12 (traction control onward) lines. |
| **Why** | **Own analysis of v0.42 first** (`runs/run_20261001_211338.csv`): time by limiter: traction-control cut active 30.8 s (cut > 0.02 in 1,497 of 3,969 steps, mean cut 0.20), full throttle 19.4 s, braking 18.6 s, riding the allowed speed 5.9 s, part throttle 5.6 s, full lock 2.8 s. Out of every slow corner the car ran in 2nd/3rd with the stored throttle at 1.0 and only 0.5–0.8 sent (e.g. the hairpin exit 3,285–3,330 m, 66 → 132 km/h, cut the whole way). The flick (~2,449–2,510 m) is ~60 m at full lock on the 75 km/h `corner_speed` floor, the hairpin full lock 75 → 64 km/h. **Inherited conclusion challenged:** "acceleration peaks at 2–2.5 m/s of over-speed" (v0.28) and the v0.42 trial note that `tc_slip` 3.0 grew the flick slide. Re-measured on v0.42 (straight, `\|steer\|` < 0.3, crest excluded): 2nd gear 10.46 m/s² at 2.5 m/s, 10.41 at 3.0; 1st still rising at 3.0–3.5 (11.2–11.35); so the peak is not sharp, and closed-loop the flat limit was clearly too low (below). The cost of a higher limit is in corners only, so the extra is tied to straight running. **Candidates, closed-loop full laps with `run_race.py` on v0.42 (TORCS s; baseline reproduced at 83.128):** (1) **Steering cap, new** (front tyres at 21° full lock look far past their peak slip angle: the kinematic radius ~7.7 m would need ~53 m/s² at 73 km/h): cap 0.9 → 83.992, 0.8 → 83.830, 0.7 → 84.694, 0.6 → 84.446, **all left the track at the hairpin exit** (`\|trackPos\|` 1.09–1.19 at ~3,292 m): with less lock the car kept 72–73 km/h into the hairpin (64 at full lock) but could not turn tight enough. Full lock is needed; the hypothesis is wrong. (2) **Throttle ramp, new** (+0.05 → more per step; 691 ramp steps per lap): +0.1 → 82.896, +0.2 → 82.858, +0.3 → 82.784, +0.5 → 82.692, +1 → 82.662 (flick `\|trackPos\|` 0.69–0.77, flick slide up to 26.6 km/h). (3) **Flat `tc_slip`** 3.0 → 82.836, 3.25 → 82.594, 3.5 → 82.432 (flick slide 25.6 km/h), 3.75 → 82.274, 4.0 → 82.272, 4.5 → 82.304 (36.2 km/h), 5.0 → 82.100, 6.0 → 82.256: the gain is real (exits from slow corners: start −0.09 s, ~1,900–2,100 m −0.24, ~3,300–3,400 m −0.11), but the flick slide grows to 25–36 km/h. (4) **Slip limit raised by the steering only** (`tc_slip` + extra·clip(1 − \|steer\|/s, 0, 1)): extra 1.0–6.0, s 0.15–1.0 → 82.03–82.62, flick 0.69–0.73, slide 16.8–20.6 km/h; best 3.5/0.7 → 82.048, which was run in full: **medium-corner exits then slid 13–19 km/h** (19.0 at ~506 m, also ~460, ~795, ~1,540, ~1,945 m; v0.42's worst outside the flick was 10.4 at the kink): as the rear steps out the wheel counter-steers toward 0, which raised the limit and fed the slide. Using the look-ahead bearing instead of the steering (`\|aim\|` 3°/5°) → 82.754/82.538. (5) **Chosen: steering-scaled extra with a slide guard** (extra also fades with sideways speed): guard 5 → 82.554, 8 → 82.328, 10 → 82.316, 12 → 82.266 (extra 3.5, 0.7); extra 2.5 → 82.532, 3.5 → 82.328, **5.0 → 82.236**, 6.0 (at 1.0) → 82.236; 5.0 at 1.0 → 82.326, 5.0 at 1.0 guard 10 → 82.142; 3.5 at 0.5 → 82.496, at 1.0 → 82.286. All on track, 0 damage, max sideways outside the flick 10.3–11.2 km/h (the kink, as in v0.42). (6) Inherited ideas re-run on top of (4) (informational): full-lock brake → 82.066 (flick 0.533, hairpin 64 → 52 km/h); `corner_speed` 80 → **left the track at the flick** (2.56 at ~2,508 m); `downshift_rpm` 15,500/16,000 → 82.214/82.010 (no gain). (5) won: a −0.89 s gain kept almost all of the flat limit's time while every watch point stayed at the v0.42 level. 5.0/0.7/8 sits mid-plateau: every guarded value from 2.5 to 6 m/s, 0.5 to 1.0 and 8 to 12 km/h ran 82.14–82.55 with nothing failing, and even a flat 6 m/s (no guards) stayed on track. **For the next agent (informational, on v0.43 as committed):** + `turn_grip` 9 → **80.704** (flick 0.840, kink 16.8 km/h); + `turn_grip` 9 + full-lock brake → **80.786** (flick 0.541, hairpin 52 km/h, kink 16.8); + `brake_aero` 0.006 → **81.058** (flick 0.762, flick slide 11.6 km/h); + throttle ramp 0.2 → 81.936 (flick 0.717); + full-lock brake → 82.352 (flick 0.528). |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (closed-loop trial, see Why): 82.236 s. Risks: wheelspin on straight exits (more rear slip at the start of each straight), slides on exits from medium corners if the guard comes too late. Reject on damage or leaving the track. |
| **Lap time** | 1:22.23 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 256 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 64 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261001_213459.csv`); TORCS time **82.236 s** → on screen 1:22:23. Lap **0.89 s faster** than v0.42 (1:23.12 → 1:22.23) — **the fastest lap so far**. Zero damage, on track (max `\|trackPos\|` 0.710 at ~2,477 m, the flick; v0.42 0.718). Gains out of slow and medium corners: start −0.16 s, ~3,300 m −0.11 (hairpin exit), ~2,500 m −0.10 (flick exit), ~2,000/2,100 m −0.06 each, ~500/1,600/1,900/2,800/3,400 m −0.04 each; small losses ~1,300 m +0.03, ~1,000/2,200 m +0.02 each. Traction-control cut active 30.8 → 17.9 s (cut steps 1,497 → 891); sent throttle > 0.95 in 29.6 → 36.3% of the lap; full throttle uncut 19.4 → 28.8 s. Braking steps 890 → 908. Flick slide 17.9 → 17.3 km/h, kink 10.4 → 10.6 km/h (the largest slide outside the flick), hairpin 64 km/h, exit −0.60 → −0.56. Top speed 256.8 km/h at ~1,394 m in 5th (unchanged). Slowest corner 64 km/h (~3,282 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — fastest lap so far (−0.89 s), watch points unchanged |
| **Learned** | Traction control was the biggest single limit (30.8 s of cut): the 2.5 m/s limit is right in corners but far too low in a straight line, where 3.5–7.5 m/s all accelerate better. Any extra must be tied to the car actually running straight: the steering alone is not enough, because a sliding car counter-steers toward 0 and the extra then feeds the slide (19 km/h at ~500 m); a sideways-speed guard fixes that for ~0.2 s of the gain. Less steering lock is not a way to carry speed into the hairpin and flick: the car needs full lock there and runs off at the hairpin exit (new hypothesis tested and rejected). The flick is still the shared limit of the corner knobs (`corner_speed` 80 still leaves the track), while `turn_grip` 9 (−1.53 s on top of v0.43) and `brake_aero` 0.006 (−1.18 s) remain the largest measured gains. |

## v0.44 — Corner grip rises with speed (downforce): `turn_grip_aero` 1.5e-4

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.44 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.44`). New knob `turn_grip_aero=1.5e-4` (line 553). The sharpness plan (corner speed from the beams, line 646) now assumes a sideways acceleration of `turn_grip·(1 + turn_grip_aero·v²)` (v in m/s) instead of a flat `turn_grip` 7: at the speed that just follows a curve of radius R, v² = `turn_grip`·R·(1 + `turn_grip_aero`·v²), so v² = `turn_grip`·R / (1 − `turn_grip`·`turn_grip_aero`·R) (denominator floored at 0.1, which only matters for radii > ~860 m, already faster than the top speed). Grip assumed: 7 m/s² at rest, 7.8 at 100 km/h, 8.8 at 150, 10.2 at 200. Comment block added (lines 633–637). Everything else unchanged; lines from 553 down moved by 1, from the sharpness-plan comment (old 632) down by 6. |
| **Why** | **Own analysis of v0.43 first** (`runs/run_20261001_213459.csv`): time by limiter: full throttle 28.8 s, braking 18.9 s, traction-control cut 17.9 s, part throttle 7.4 s, riding the allowed speed 6.3 s, full lock 2.8 s. Section times show the lap is now mostly corners: 400, 700, 1000, 1500, 1900, 2600–3000 m (92–165 km/h) and the flick/hairpin. A closed-loop `turn_grip` 9 run (80.704) gained most in the **fast** corners (minimum speed ~1,200 m 209 → 228 km/h, ~600 m 171 → 182, ~900 m 133 → 144, ~100–200 m 148 → 163), little in the slow ones, and lost 0.06 s at the flick: grip binds where the car is fast. That fits downforce: braking per unit of pedal rises ~40% from 80 to 210 km/h (v0.40 measurement) and the braking plan already assumes 14 → 26 m/s² from rest to 200 km/h (`brake_aero`, a relative rise of 2.9e-4 per (m/s)²), while the sharpness plan assumed the same grip at every speed. **Inherited conclusions challenged:** (a) "`turn_grip` 9 is the strongest open idea" (plan ranking): a speed-dependent grip is as fast or faster at every setting from 1.25e-4; (b) "`turn_steer_max` 0.6 protects the flick" (v0.27): the flick approach (~2,433–2,449 m) is driven at `\|steer\|` 0.37–0.59 under braking, i.e. inside the sharpness plan, and that is where every grip raise adds entry speed and sideways slide (`turn_grip` 9: 2,438–2,449 m sideways −4 → −15 km/h before full lock); at 0.5 the flick drops from 0.84 to 0.66. **Candidates, closed-loop full laps with `run_race.py` on v0.43 (TORCS s; baseline reproduced at 82.236); figures are max `\|trackPos\|` at the flick and kink sideways speed:** (1) **`turn_grip` flat** 8 → 81.412 (0.804, 11.3 km/h); 8.5 → 81.038 (0.819, 15.0); 9 → 80.704 (0.840, 16.8); 9.5 → 80.890 (0.847, 18.6). (2) **New: `turn_grip_aero`** (`turn_grip` 7) 5e-5 → 81.650 (0.713, 11.3: all watch points at the v0.43 level); 1e-4 → 81.038 (0.852, 18.2); 1.25e-4 → 80.774 (0.831, 14.6); **1.5e-4 → 80.588** (0.852, 17.3); 1.75e-4 → 80.596 (0.833, 14.3); 2e-4 → 80.702 (0.694, 17.4); 2.5e-4 → 80.678 (0.856, 16.9); 3e-4 → 80.622 (0.864, 14.9). The gain saturates at ~1.5e-4 (other limits bind beyond it); the flick reads 0.69–0.86 almost at random once grip rises (it depends on how the car leaves the kink), not in step with the value. (3) **`turn_steer_max`** (enabling for the flick) 0.5 → 82.466 (flick 0.620, +0.23 s); 0.45 → 82.630 (0.636). With `turn_grip` 9: 0.5 → 80.922 (0.66, kink 17.2); 0.45 → 81.136 (0.70); 0.4 → 81.722 (0.68). With `turn_grip_aero`: 1e-4 → 81.148 (0.68, kink 18.8); 1.5e-4 → 80.966 (0.72); 2e-4 → 80.680 (0.69, kink 14.6); 3e-4 → 80.650 (0.75). (2) won: the fastest single change measured (−1.65 s), physically grounded (half the relative aero rise the braking plan already assumes, matching the ~40% measured from braking), and one knob. 1.5e-4 is the fastest value and its neighbours (1.25e-4–3e-4) all finished between 80.59 and 80.77 with 0 damage and the flick ≤ 0.86. (1) is the same lever with more grip at low speed, where it is not needed; (3) protects the flick but costs 0.2–0.4 s and is a separate mechanism, left for a later version. **For the next agent (informational, on v0.44 as committed):** + throttle ramp +0.2/step → 80.620 (flick 0.83, flick slide 24.4 km/h, hairpin exit 0.73); + `turn_steer_max` 0.5 → 80.966 (flick 0.72); + full-lock brake (pedal ≥ 0.05, throttle 0 above `\|steer\|` 0.95 and 50 km/h) → 80.708 (flick **0.546**, hairpin 64 → 52 km/h, hairpin exit 0.44); + `brake_aero` 0.006 → **left the track at the flick** (1.93 at ~2,467 m). |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (closed-loop trial, see Why): 80.588 s. Risks: the flick (`\|trackPos\|` up to ~0.86 in the trials) and the kink (~2,385 m, 14–18 km/h sideways under braking in every grip raise), the hairpin exit (~0.67–0.70 in the trials). Reject on damage or leaving the track. |
| **Lap time** | 1:20.58 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 257 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 64 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261001_215050.csv`); TORCS time **80.588 s** → on screen 1:20:58. Lap **1.65 s faster** than v0.43 (1:22.23 → 1:20.58) — **the fastest lap so far**. Zero damage, on track (max `\|trackPos\|` **0.852** at ~2,477 m, the flick; v0.43 0.710). Gains in the fast and medium corners: ~2,300 m −0.18 s (kink), ~2,600 m −0.16, ~1,200/2,700/2,900 m −0.13 each, ~100/200/600/1,000 m −0.11 each, ~1,500 m −0.10, ~1,400 m −0.08, ~700/3,000 m −0.07 each, ~300 m −0.06; losses ~500 m +0.10 (exit of the ~400 m corner, minimum 101 → 98 km/h) and ~2,400 m +0.06 (flick). Corner minimum speeds: ~1,200 m 209 → 228 km/h, ~600 m 171 → 183, ~900 m 133 → 143, ~100–200 m 148 → 164, ~2,600/2,700 m 130 → 137/138, ~3,000 m 120 → 128, ~1,000 m 121 → 128. Braking steps 908 → 835; traction-control cut 17.9 → 18.3 s; part throttle 7.4 → 9.1 s. **Watch points:** kink (~2,385 m) sideways 10.6 → **17.3 km/h** (braking at `\|steer\|` 0.25–0.37 from ~220 km/h, `\|trackPos\|` 0.33); flick slide 17.3 → 22.5 km/h, `\|trackPos\|` 0.71 → 0.85; hairpin 64 km/h, exit −0.56 → −0.67; largest slide in the other corners 8.4 → 9.5 km/h (~790 m). Top speed 257.4 km/h at ~1,370 m. Slowest corner 64 km/h (~3,282 m, the hairpin). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — fastest lap so far (−1.65 s); flick margin 0.29 → 0.15, see Learned |
| **Learned** | The sharpness plan was too cautious in fast corners: grip there rises with downforce, and a speed-dependent grip (+46% at 200 km/h, half of what the braking plan already assumes) gained 1.65 s, more than any flat `turn_grip`. The flick and the kink are now the clear limits: every grip raise, flat or speed-dependent, carries more speed out of the kink into the long brake for the flick, so the kink slides 14–18 km/h under braking while turning and the flick runs out to ~0.83–0.86. The flick's width is set mostly by the approach (~2,433–2,449 m, braking at `\|steer\|` 0.4–0.6, inside the sharpness plan) and swings 0.69–0.86 between nearby settings, so a single run's flick value is not a reliable margin. Next: an enabling change for the flick (full-lock brake 0.55, or `turn_steer_max` 0.5 at 0.72; both measured on v0.44), then the throttle ramp or further braking knobs on top; `brake_aero` 0.006 now leaves the track at the flick. |
