## v0.81 — Exit run-out: the inside target lets go once the plan has headroom

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.81 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.81`). One mechanism, the **exit run-out** (new; lines 702–716, knobs at 566–568). On the inside half of a bend (phase < 0), after the v0.61 exit release, the inside target is multiplied by 1 − `c.run`, where `c.run` is the smoothed share clip((allowed speed of the previous step − speed − `run_head`)/`run_width`, 0, 1): nothing while the car is within `run_head` **10** km/h of the plan, all of the inside target released from 25 km/h of headroom (`run_width` **15**), low-passed with `run_lp` **0.8** per step (reset to 0 on the outside half of a bend). The target goes to the centre, not to the outside; the inside integral is kept. No other value changed (`rel_start` 7, `rel_width` 2, `rel_share` 0.8 unchanged). |
| **Why** | **Own analysis of v0.80 first** (`runs/run_20261003_011448.csv`, 15 m step trace of the whole lap, `tools/width.py`, `tools/patterns.py`). (a) **Exits:** out of the medium bends the car is already below the plan while the line still steers it to the inside: 1,544–1,591 m speed 140/147/156/166 against an allowed 146/165/194/367 km/h, steer +0.34 to +0.42, slide 10–16 km/h, sent throttle 0.77–0.91 (traction control cutting), `trackPos` +0.38 → +0.21 (inside half; 1.2 units of road unused); the logged target is still +0.59 to +0.76 inside there. Same at 3,000–3,015 m (151/158 vs 180/367, steer −0.38, slide 16.3, throttle 0.66) and 1,063 m (161 vs 297, slide 15.2, throttle 0.83). Steering budget at 1,560 m: heading term −0.35, line pull +0.25, integral up to +0.4: the inside pull is what holds the lock on. The v0.61 release waits for the road along the track direction to grow 7 m, which comes late while the car is yawed into the bend. (b) **Entries:** between the set-up's end (road 80 m) and bend detection (bearing > 2°, road ~35 m) the target is 0 and the car drifts back to the centre (452 m: −0.23 → −0.06); at detection the phase is already −1, so the car turns straight in. (c) **Flick:** the car arrives 30–40 km/h over the plan at 2,370–2,432 m (232 vs 194, 208 vs 170, 182 vs 156) with ABS cutting, runs wide out of the right kink to +0.46 and so enters the left arc from the inside; 2,432–2,523 m takes 4.4 s. **Candidates (own):** (1) **exit run-out from the plan's headroom** (new, not in the plan or changelog) — chosen; (2) the same with an **outside exit target** (take the car out, the brief's "nothing takes the car out"); (3) the run-out also freeing the inside integral; (4) **speed-dependent look-ahead exponent** (orchestrator's note): 2 + e·clip((v − 120)/80, 0, 1); (5) path-following set-up (the brief's proposal) — not built: referencing the heading and look-ahead terms to a desired heading θ is algebraically a pull of 6.8·θ steer, i.e. v0.80's capped pull with another cap, and v0.80 measured caps 0.12–0.30 as chaotic at the flick (re-checked here on v0.81: `setup_pull` 0.2/0.25 suite 1 74.674/74.718, max 0.928/0.970); the wide entry is limited by the 80 m of sight between `setup_dist` and `setup_road`, not by the controller's form; (6) flick entered from the right (needs its own version). **Inherited conclusions challenged:** "unused exit width with full throttle applied is not worth time" — true where the throttle is full, but on these exits the sent throttle is cut and the steering is held by the line, so letting go is worth time; "the wide entry needs path following" (v0.80) — see (5). **Trials** (variant file, `tools/suite.py`, ~970 races; v0.80 reference suite 1 74.841, all-30 74.833): unsmoothed release (suite 1): headroom 5 → 75.509, 1 of 10 off; 10 → 75.138, 1 of 10 off (`turn_grip` +0.5, 1.068); with the integral freed 75.208, 1 off; smoothed 0.8 with the integral freed 74.836–74.839; **smoothed 0.8, integral kept 74.650**; smoothing 0.6/0.9 (integral freed) 75.030/74.874. All 30 (smoothed, integral kept): `run_head` 7 → 74.651 (max 0.853); **10 → 74.652–74.654 (max 0.845–0.859; chosen)**; 15 → 74.688; 20 → 74.708; `run_width` 10 → 74.706, 20 → 74.652, 30 → 74.615 but max 0.971; `run_lp` 0.7 → 74.761, 0.85 → 74.632 but max 0.931 (shifted max 0.983); integral half freed 74.750. A latch (run-out only after the car was plan-limited in this bend) changed nothing (74.652 vs 74.654) and was left out. (2) outside exit target: share 0.2 of `line_offset` → all-30 74.868; 0.3/0.6 → suite 1 74.880/75.288; unsmoothed 0.5/1.0 → 76.223/78.760: taking the car out costs time, letting it go gains time. (4) exponent e 0.2/0.4 → suite 1 74.929/74.895, max 0.886/0.978: lost. |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (`tools/suite.py` on the final code): all-30 74.654 (−0.179 s vs v0.80), 0 of 30 off, max 0.770/0.859/0.760; shifted bases 0 of 40 off. |
| **Lap time** | 1:14.49 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 283 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 59 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261003_013150.csv`, `tools/finalize.py`); TORCS time **74.494 s** → on screen 1:14:49, **new best and below the 74.72 target** (v0.80 74.728, −0.234 s; largest section changes: 2,500 m −0.140, 2,300 m +0.060, 3,300 m −0.044, 800 m −0.044, 2,600 m −0.040, 1,200 m +0.022). Max \|trackPos\| 0.705 at ~2,477 m (flick; v0.80 0.763); hairpin 58.6 km/h, 0.67, exit slide 13.2 km/h; flick slide 13.4, exit 10.4; kink 10.0; largest slide elsewhere 17.0 km/h at 3,010 m. **Suites** (3×10): 74.648 / 74.673 / 74.641, **all-30 74.654** (v0.80 74.833), max 0.770 / 0.859 / 0.760, **0 of 30 off**. **Shifted bases** (suites 1–2): `brake_margin` 14 → 74.640/74.617, max 0.964 (`brake_margin` −2, hairpin), 0 of 20 off; `line_offset` 0.5 → 74.583/74.707, max 0.935, 0 of 20 off: **0 of 40 off**. **Pattern line** (v0.80 → v0.81): steering reversals 81 → **68**, oscillation episodes 5 → **4**, time in them 2.0 → **1.6 s**; target flips 108 → 108; allowed-speed jumps > 20 km/h 92 → 84; brake applications 34 → 36 (short touches 15 → 16, re-applied within 10 steps 9 → 11); shifts 39 → 39 (1 undone); TC cut 11.9 → 11.5 s. Remaining episodes: 535–545 m, 1,456–1,480 m, 1,517–1,538 m, 1,902–1,915 m. **Width** (`tools/width.py`, entry −80 m / apex / exit +80 m, + = outside; v0.80 → v0.81): 452 m +0.20/+0.26/+0.34 → +0.23/+0.29/+0.34; 783 m +0.17/−0.09/+0.30 → +0.18/−0.06/+0.28; 1,040 m +0.40/−0.44/+0.16 → +0.40/−0.44/+0.16; 1,530 m +0.10/−0.44/−0.11 → +0.11/−0.45/−0.07; 1,931 m +0.31/−0.20/−0.02 → +0.31/−0.20/+0.02; flick −0.09/+0.61/−0.14 → −0.39/+0.69/−0.06 (apex found 10 m later, 2,463 → 2,473 m); 2,986 m +0.17/−0.43/+0.04 → +0.16/−0.43/−0.00; hairpin +0.23/+0.62/+0.47 → +0.27/+0.63/+0.50. **The width table is practically unchanged** (exits within ±0.04): the gain is time, not a visibly wider line. **Follow-ups measured on v0.81** (not in this version): all 30: `rel_start` 5 → 74.616, `rel_share` 0 (v0.61 release off) → 74.627, `rel_start` 5 + `rel_width` 4 → 74.627, `setup_road` 70 → 74.655 (max 0.875); suite 1 (v0.81 74.648): `corner_speed` 79 → 74.625 (max 0.917), `rel_share` 1 → 74.741, `setup_pull` 0.2/0.25 → 74.674/74.718 (max 0.928/0.970). All within the ~0.04 s noise of a suite mean. |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — new best lap (74.494, below the 74.72 target) and all-30 74.833 → 74.654 (−0.179 s), 0 of 30 off, suite maximum 0.840 → 0.859; shifted bases 0 of 40 off (max 0.964); no oscillation brought back (2.0 → 1.6 s). |
| **Learned** | On exits the inside line target was costing drive: once the plan allows 10–25 km/h more than the car's speed the bend no longer limits the car, and dropping the inside pull there is worth ~0.18 s over 30 perturbed laps. The signal must be smoothed (the allowed speed jumps > 20 km/h 84–92 times a lap; unsmoothed the release was slower and 1 of 10 off), and the inside integral must be kept (freeing it: +0.1 to +0.2 s). **Letting go is faster, pushing out is slower:** any outside exit target (0.2–1.0 of `line_offset`) lost 0.2–4 s, so the exit positions (+0.0 to +0.3 at +80 m) barely move and the width table is unchanged: the car's exit path is set by its speed and yaw at the apex, not by the target. The line theme is therefore still open at the entries and the flick: the set-up has only the 80 m between `setup_dist` 160 and `setup_road` 80 to move the car (≈ 1.25 s at 230 km/h; 0.02 rad of yaw gives ~0.25 of `trackPos`), then the target is 0 until the bend is detected ~45 m later and the car drifts back; a path-following form of the set-up is the same capped pull in other units. The largest single block of time is the flick (2,370–2,523 m ≈ 6 s): the car arrives 30–40 km/h over the plan, runs wide out of the right kink and enters the left arc from the inside (entry −0.39 at −80 m, on the wrong side). With v0.81 the v0.61 road-growth release is nearly redundant (`rel_share` 0: 74.627). |

## v0.82 — Corner set-up ends a little earlier (`setup_road` 80 → 85); the gap-free hand-over measured and lost

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.82 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.82`). One knob of the corner set-up, changed in place on line 559: `setup_road` 80 → **85** m (the held set-up of v0.80 pulls to the outside while more than this much road is visible along the track direction). No other value, no new code, no line moved. |
| **Why** | **Own analysis of v0.81 first** (`runs/run_20261003_013150.csv`: step traces of 300–470 m, 1,400–1,545 m and 2,240–2,540 m, `tools/width.py`, `tools/patterns.py`). (a) **The gap before turn-in is small in distance, not the cause of the narrow entry:** at 452 m the set-up moves the car only −0.10 → −0.26 between 301 and 347 m (pull capped at 0.15 steer, ~1 s at 255 km/h), and in the gap it comes back to −0.13 by 399 m; at 1,530 m −0.12 → −0.03. The entry is narrow because the set-up has only ~1 s to act, not because of the 45 m without a target. (b) **The turn-in is an early apex:** at detection (399 m, 145 km/h) the inside pull plus the inside integral (saturates in ~0.3 s) yaw the car 10.5° into the bend within 8 m; it reaches +0.13 inside at 415 m, 37 m before the slowest point, then washes out to −0.33 at 456 m and −0.42 at 464 m with 9–14 km/h of slide, riding the plan (allowed 102–114 km/h from 20–26 m of sight). (c) **Flick:** the right kink (2,372–2,392 m, steer −0.7, 30 km/h over the plan with the ABS cutting) carries the car from −0.18 to +0.48, the inside of the left arc; the arc then runs at full lock from +0.39 to −0.70 at 80 → 65 km/h. Between 2,286 and 2,366 m the plan flickers (one full-brake step at 2,293 m, allowed 233–272), worth an estimated ~0.05 s only, because the car is over the plan from 2,366 m anyway. **Candidates (own):** (1) **gap-free hand-over**: the set-up held down to 30–60 m of road, i.e. until the bend is detected (the orchestrator's finding 1, untested in this form); (2) **new: bend detection on the track-relative bearing** (`aim` minus the track direction, so the car's own yaw toward the outside does not bring the detection forward), alone and with (1); (3) **new: softer turn-in**, the inside line pull capped (0.2/0.3 steer) and the inside integral halved, against the early apex of (b); (4) later or earlier inside phase (centre 40–70 m of road instead of 60) and detection threshold 1.5–3°; (5) the set-up's end point `setup_road` 85–100. **Trials (~860 races; all on v0.81, base all-30 74.654).** (1) all 30: `setup_road` 60/50/40/30 → **74.679 / 75.111 / 74.960 / 74.990** (0 of 30 off, max 0.905–0.915); with `setup_dist` 200 75.201; with `setup_pull` 0.25 74.964, 0.10 75.178; `setup_min` 2 75.129. Section diff at 40 m (single lap 75.002): 400 m +0.140, 800 m +0.132, 1,500 m +0.146 s. Cause seen in the trace: the held pull keeps the nose ~0.8° toward the outside, which makes the look-ahead bearing larger, so the bend is detected at 372 m instead of 399 m (53 m of road instead of 33 m); the car turns in earlier and faster (156 km/h at 10.5° of yaw), is 27 km/h over the plan at 405 m and washes out to −0.51 (slowest 92 km/h instead of 102); the entry itself moved only −0.24 → −0.30. (2) suite 1 (base 74.648): track-relative detection alone at 2/2.5/3° → 75.464 (max 0.995) / 75.222 / 75.068; with the set-up held to 40 m at 2.5/3/3.5° → 74.847 / **74.811** / 74.804, to 60 m 75.152; all 30 at 3°/40 m: **74.850**, 0 of 30 off, max 0.972; with `setup_pull` 0.25: 1 of 10 off. So even with the early detection removed the hand-over is 0.2 s slower. (3) suite 1: pull cap 0.3 → 74.704, 0.2 → 74.948, 0.3 with half the integral → 75.118; integral ×1.3/×1.5 → 74.622 / 74.735. (4) suite 1: phase centre 70 → 74.899, 65 (width 15) → 74.730, 50 → 74.910, 40 (width 10) → 75.484; detection threshold 1.5/1.75/2.25/3° → 74.999 / 74.672 / 74.593 (max 0.939) / 74.894 (max 0.997). (5) all 30: `setup_road` **85 → 74.619** (max 0.855; shifted bases 0 of 40 off, max 0.990), 90 → 74.599 (max 0.844) but shifted bases 1 of 40 off (`brake_margin` 14 with `brake_margin` −2, 1.189), 95 → 74.641 (max 0.856; shifted 1 of 40 off, 1.288); 100 → suite 1 74.613. **Chosen: 85**, the only neighbour at or below the base on all 30 with 0 of 40 off on the shifted bases; 90 is 0.02 s faster on the mean but spends the shifted-base margin. **Inherited conclusions challenged:** the handed-down hypothesis "the 45 m gap lets the car drift back and a gap-free hand-over is the next line step" is false as a gain: every form of it is slower, and the measured direction is the opposite (a slightly shorter set-up is as fast or faster). v0.80's "85 is 1 of 40 off on the shifted bases" no longer holds on the v0.81 car (0 of 40), which is why 85 can come back. Not tried for time: the flick-specific step (holding right through the kink) and the v0.75 mechanism on v0.81. |

| **Min speed** | 58 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261003_014604.csv`, `tools/finalize.py`); TORCS time **74.454 s** → on screen 1:14:45 (v0.81 74.494, −0.040 s; largest section changes: 1,900 m −0.040, 3,300 m +0.022, 200 m −0.022). Damage 0, top 283 km/h, slowest corner 58 km/h at ~3,277 m (hairpin). Max \|trackPos\| 0.776 at ~2,474 m (flick; v0.81 0.705), hairpin 0.66; slides: flick 15.9 km/h (exit 11.8), kink 9.0, hairpin exit 12.2, elsewhere 17.5 at ~3,006 m. **Suites:** 74.643 / 74.621 / 74.592, **all-30 74.619** (v0.81 74.654), max 0.776 / 0.855 / 0.778, **0 of 30 off**. **Shifted bases** (suites 1–2, 40 runs): `brake_margin` 14 → 74.676, max 0.990; `line_offset` 0.5 → 74.582, max 0.940; **0 of 40 off**. **Width** (`tools/width.py`; entry −80 m / apex / exit +80 m, + = outside), v0.81 → v0.82: 452 m +0.23/+0.29/+0.34 → +0.21/+0.28/+0.42; 783 m +0.18/−0.06/+0.28 → +0.18/−0.07/+0.26; 1,040 m +0.40/−0.44/+0.16 → +0.36/−0.48/+0.17; 1,530 m +0.11/−0.45/−0.07 → +0.08/−0.40/−0.01; 1,931 m +0.31/−0.20/+0.02 → +0.30/−0.17/+0.07; flick −0.39/+0.69/−0.06 → −0.36/+0.77/−0.05; 2,986 m +0.16/−0.43/−0.00 → +0.14/−0.43/+0.16; hairpin +0.27/+0.63/+0.50 → +0.10/+0.61/+0.49. **The line is not better:** entries are 0.01–0.04 less far outside (hairpin 0.17), exits 0.01–0.16 wider at 452 m, 1,530 m, 1,931 m and 2,986 m. **Patterns** (`tools/patterns.py`), v0.81 → v0.82: steering reversals 68 → 65, episodes 4 → 1 (1.6 → 0.5 s); target flips 108 → 112; allowed-speed jumps > 20 km/h 84 → 90; brake applications 36 → 36 (short touches 16 → 17, re-applied within 10 steps 11 → 10); 39 shifts, 1 undone; TC cut 11.5 → 11.3 s. |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — all-30 74.654 → 74.619 (−0.035 s, noise level), 0 of 30 off, suite maximum 0.859 → 0.855, shifted bases 0 of 40 off (max 0.964 → 0.990), best single lap 74.454; no oscillation brought back (1.6 → 0.5 s). A knob nudge, not a line improvement: the line mechanisms tried this iteration all lost. |
| **Learned** | The outside set-up and the turn-in are coupled through the car's yaw: a set-up pull held closer to the bend keeps the nose toward the outside, the look-ahead bearing passes 2° some 25 m sooner, and the inside pull then turns the car in earlier and faster than the plan can slow it (452 m: 27 km/h over, slowest point 102 → 92 km/h). With the detection made independent of the yaw the hand-over is still 0.2 s slower, so a wider entry by itself does not pay in this controller. The turn-in is an early apex (inside reached ~35 m before the slowest point, then a wash-out to the outside), and every softer or later turn-in measured is slower too; a likely reason (not isolated) is that the corner speed is the plan's (sight distance), not the line's. The remaining line gain therefore probably needs the plan and the line changed together (an allowed speed that credits the radius of the line the car is on), not another pull: a hypothesis for the next agent. `setup_road` 85–95 is a flat plateau on the mean (74.60–74.64); the shifted bases decide (85: 0 of 40; 90, 95: 1 of 40). `tools/suite.py` note: `--set` appeared to be ignored when `--cfg` is given (a shifted-base run with `--set setup_road=90` reproduced v0.81's numbers; put the knob inside each `--cfg` or in the driver). Open for the next agent: the flick-specific step (stay right through the 2,372–2,392 m kink) and the v0.75 mechanism were not re-measured on v0.81. |

## v0.83 — Traction control on the slip ratio (`tc_vref` 110)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.83 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.83`). One mechanism, **slip-ratio traction control** (new; knob at line 588, code at lines 919–926). The rear over-speed limit (`tc_slip` + the straight extra) is multiplied by speed / `tc_vref` (new knob, **110** km/h), so it is a share of the car's speed instead of a fixed m/s: turning, 2.5 m/s at 60 km/h, 4.5 at 110, 6.1 at 150. `launch_slip` (launch and straight exits) is added after it, unchanged. No other value changed. Developed by a parallel agent (α) on a private copy of v0.82 and applied by the orchestrator (batch 9 runs three agents in parallel; the harness now shares its 4 race slots machine-wide, commit `b8fe126`). |
| **Why** | **Own analysis of v0.82 first** (`runs/run_20261003_014604.csv`, step traces of the four exits, and the simulator's tyre code `simuv2/wheel.cpp`). Orchestrator's reading: 400–600 m takes 5.94 s with 3.5 s of traction-control cut, the largest traction-limited stretch of the lap. Agent's trace: at 460–530 m the traction control is not a sawtooth; it holds a steady cut of 0.3–0.47 (over-speed ~4.3 m/s at 105–123 km/h, slide 13–16 km/h, only 3–6 m/s² of acceleration); the sawtooth at 425–457 m is the throttle riding the allowed speed. The tyre force in the simulator depends on the combined slip s = √(sx² + sy²), sx = over-speed / speed, sy = sin(slip angle); it is flat from s ≈ 0.15 (peak 0.20) and shared as sx/s (drive) and sy/s (cornering): in a bend more wheelspin adds no drive, only slide. A fixed 4.5 m/s is 0.27 of the speed at 60 km/h (hairpin and flick exits) and 0.11 at 150. **Candidates and trials** (private variants, `tools/suite.py`, ~640 races; all-30 unless noted; base 74.619): (A) **slip-ratio limit**, `tc_vref` 100 / 105 / **110** / 115 / 120 / 130 → 74.595 / 74.611 / **74.545** / 74.565 / 74.597 (max 0.930) / 74.628; 90 → suite 1 74.734 (base 74.643); 110 with `tc_slip` 4.0 / 5.0 → 74.614 / 74.567; 110 with `tc_slip_straight` 6.5 → 74.631. (B) **clutch pedal on the upshift step** (new: for the 0.05 s shift time the simulator opens the clutch and caps the throttle at 0.1 while the pedal is below 0.01, and the pedal on that step was set from the old gear's wheel rpm, i.e. 0) → 74.599 alone, 74.496 with (A): a second mechanism, measured again after v0.84. (C) **ground-rpm upshift gate** (open idea 4) 14,000 / 17,500 → 74.620 / 74.611, 16,000 → suite 1 74.624: flat on the clutch car too. (D) **lower gear on the exits** (open idea 5), `lowest_running_gear` 1 → suite 1 74.681, flick 0.926: 2nd gear with the clutch slipped already delivers more force (~13.9 kN) than the tyres pass (~9–10 kN). **Inherited conclusion challenged:** v0.46's "slip-ratio traction control, no gain" (80.28–81.41 on v0.45): on today's car every reference speed from 100 to 120 km/h is at or below the base. **Alternatives considered by the parallel agents on v0.82** (not in this version): flick line step in the steering block (agent β, ~450 races): no outside line target while above the plan, `plan_out` 0 / −0.5 / −1 → all-30 74.589 / 74.608 / 74.595 (noise; shifted bases 1 of 40 off; flick entry unchanged at −0.35: with 0.2 more right steer in the kink the path is the same to 0.01 `trackPos`, the fronts are saturated 20+ km/h over the plan on the crest); short-range set-up below `setup_road` → suite 1 74.864 / 75.496 (3 of 10 off); inside set-up for an open bend → suite 1 74.609–74.736: rejected, no version. Slip reference in the sharpness plan (agent γ): all-30 74.577 alone; it is the next version (v0.84). |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (`tools/suite.py`, re-measured by the orchestrator on the final code): all-30 74.545 (−0.074 s vs v0.82), 0 of 30 off, max 0.760/0.851/0.762; shifted bases 0 of 40 off. |
| **Lap time** | 1:14.50 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 283 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 58 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261003_030404.csv`, `tools/finalize.py`); TORCS time **74.506 s** → on screen 1:14:50 (v0.82 74.454: the unperturbed lap is **0.052 s slower**; largest section changes: 2,700 m +0.060, 2,500 m +0.040, 1,500 m −0.036). Damage 0, top 283 km/h, slowest corner 58 km/h at ~3,277 m (hairpin). Max \|trackPos\| 0.735 at ~2,476 m (flick; v0.82 0.776), hairpin 0.63; slides: flick 15.7 km/h (exit 11.8 → 7.1), kink 10.2, hairpin exit 12.2 → 8.8, elsewhere 17.5 → 21.6 at ~3,007 m (worse). **Suites:** 74.523 / 74.557 / 74.554, **all-30 74.545** (v0.82 74.619), max 0.760 / 0.851 / 0.762, **0 of 30 off**. **Shifted bases** (suites 1–2, 40 runs): `brake_margin` 14 → 74.576, max 0.974; `line_offset` 0.5 → 74.557, max 0.967; **0 of 40 off** (v0.82 max 0.990). **Patterns** (`tools/patterns.py`), v0.82 → v0.83: steering reversals 65 → 67, episodes 1 → 3 (0.5 → 1.2 s; new at 536–547 m and 1,457–1,480 m, the latter under braking; absent again on the laps with the upshift-clutch or slip-reference changes on top, so read as chaotic variation, not traced); target flips 112 → 106; allowed-speed jumps > 20 km/h 90 → 95; brake applications 36 → 38 (short touches 17 → 19, re-applied within 10 steps 10 → 11); 39 shifts, 1 undone; TC cut 11.3 → 10.8 s. **Width** (`tools/width.py`; entry −80 m / apex / exit +80 m): practically unchanged (780 m exit +0.26 → +0.34, 2,982 m exit +0.16 → +0.03, hairpin entry +0.10 → +0.22, flick −0.37 / +0.73 / −0.06). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — all-30 74.619 → 74.545 (−0.074 s, about twice the noise of a suite mean), 0 of 30 off, suite maximum 0.855 → 0.851, shifted bases 0 of 40 off (max 0.990 → 0.974): time and margin together. The single unperturbed lap is slower (74.506 vs 74.454), so the best single lap stays v0.82's 1:14.45 for now. |
| **Learned** | The cornering exits are limited by the tyre's friction circle, not by the drivetrain: a lower gear, a later upshift and more slip add nothing there, and the limit belongs on the slip ratio. The gain is small and the bowl shallow (`tc_vref` 100–120: −0.02 to −0.07 s); it also lowers the slide on the two slow exits (flick 11.8 → 7.1, hairpin 12.2 → 8.8 km/h), which is margin. Sensor finding: `S['rpm']` reads 4.7 % above the true engine rpm (rpm / `axle_rpm` is a constant 1.046 with the clutch closed), so `downshift_rpm` 15,000 is really ~14,300 and the "engine above the limiter on 17 % of full-throttle steps" reading (open idea 6) was a scale error, not lost torque. Each upshift loses one step of drive because the clutch pedal is 0 on the shift step (mechanism B). From the flick agent: the flick entry cannot be fixed from the steering block; the kink is taken 20+ km/h over the plan with the fronts saturated and the left arc is visible only ~35 m before turn-in, so the next flick step is the braking plan on its approach. Parallel workflow: three agents on private variants shared 4 race slots; every agent number was reproduced exactly by the orchestrator on the committed base. |

## v0.84 — Slip reference in the sharpness plan (`slip_ref` 0.3, `turn_grip` 7.0 → 8.0)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.84 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.84`). One mechanism, the **slip reference** in the braking plan's sharpness part (new; knob at line 583, code at lines 807–821), with its grip constant co-tuned. The sharpness plan measures each beam's angle from the nose turned by `slip_ref` **0.3** of the slip angle (atan(`speedY`/`speedX`)) toward the direction of travel, in the arc radius and in the "curve would leave the track" check; beams within 0.25° of the shifted zero are skipped. `turn_grip` 7.0 → **8.0** (line 575). A sliding car is allowed less speed, a car that grips more. Stacks on v0.83; developed independently in parallel (agent γ) on a private copy of v0.82 and applied by the orchestrator. |
| **Why** | **Own analysis of v0.82 first** (agent γ: the plan recomputed offline from the beams of `runs/run_20261003_014604.csv`, matching the `allowed` column). In every medium bend the binding beam is the ±7° one: brake-limited on entry and in the first half, grip-limited once the smoothed steer passes ~0.28, the two limits within ~3 % of each other. The car slides 6–16 km/h at 120–150 km/h there (2.5–7° of slip against a 7° beam), and the plan assumed the arc onto a beam starts along the nose, so the true arc is tighter than planned whenever the car slides. **Inherited conclusion challenged:** v0.82's "corner speed is set by the plan's sight distance, not by the line" is half right: the lateral acceleration at the slowest points (centre-line radii from the TORCS track file, offline analysis only, positions approximate) is 2.0 g at 453 m, 2.45 g at 782 m, 2.66 g at 1,041 m, 2.67 g at 1,534 m, 3.2 g at 2,671 m: the car is at its grip limit there, and raising or lowering the plan's entry speed or its grip alone gains nothing. **Alternatives (agent γ, ~600 races on v0.82; suite 1, base 74.643):** new **receding-sight credit** (a beam's braking distance stretched when its end moves away as fast as the car comes), `sight_gain` 0.05 / 0.15 / 0.3 / 0.5 → 74.694 / 74.847 / 75.177 (1 off) / 10 of 10 off, and −0.1 / −0.2 → 74.957 / 75.533 (faster entries cost 10–13 km/h of minimum speed at 1,100 m and 3,000 m; slower entries lose too: the brake limit is well calibrated); `grip_boost` 0.2 / 0.4 → 74.686 / 74.607; `turn_grip` 6.5 / 7.5 / 8.0 without the slip reference → 74.945 / 74.641 / 74.608; `boost_steer` 0.15 / 0.25 → 74.696 / 74.601; `boost_fade` 0.2 → 74.600 (max 0.857); plan de-noising not repeated (lost in v0.56, v0.73, v0.74). **Slip reference, on v0.82** (ridge at `turn_grip` ≈ 7 + 3.5·`slip_ref`; all-30, base 74.619): 0.2 / 7.6 → 74.580 (shifted 1 of 40 off), **0.3 / 8.0 → 74.577** (shifted 0 of 40, max 0.995), 0.4 / 8.4 → 74.572, 0.45 / 8.8 → 74.564 (its neighbours `turn_grip` 9.2 and `grip_boost` 0.4: 1 of 20 off each); 0.6 / 9.6 → 74.912; 1.0 with 9 / 10 / 11 → 75.156 / 2 off / 3 off; off the ridge 0.3 / 7.0 → 74.882, −0.3 / 7.0 → 74.629. **Interaction check (orchestrator, all-30 on merged variants; v0.82 74.619):** slip-ratio TC alone (v0.83) 74.545; slip reference alone 74.577; **both 74.476** (sum of gains predicts 74.503: additive and a little more); upshift clutch (v0.83's mechanism B) alone 74.599, with the TC 74.496, with the slip reference 74.570, **all three 74.483** (0.007 s slower than v0.83 + slip reference, max 0.874): the upshift clutch is flat on top and was not applied (below the 0.02 s threshold of the parallel protocol). |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (`tools/suite.py` on the final code): all-30 74.476 (−0.069 s vs v0.83), 0 of 30 off, max 0.775/0.853/0.729; shifted bases 1 of 40 off. |

| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 283 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 58 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261003_030538.csv`, `tools/finalize.py`); TORCS time **74.378 s** → on screen 1:14:37, **new best single lap** (v0.83 74.506, −0.128 s; v0.82 74.454, −0.076 s; largest section changes vs v0.83: 2,700 m −0.060, 2,300 m −0.040, 2,500 m −0.040, 2,800 m −0.040, 1,500 m +0.040). Damage 0, top 283 km/h, slowest corner 58 km/h at ~3,276 m (hairpin). Max \|trackPos\| 0.724 at ~2,476 m (flick; v0.83 0.735), hairpin 0.63; slides: flick 15.7 km/h (exit 7.6), kink 9.6, hairpin exit 8.9, elsewhere 21.6 at ~3,007 m. **Suites:** 74.414 / 74.495 / 74.519, **all-30 74.476** (v0.83 74.545), max 0.775 / 0.853 / 0.729, **0 of 30 off**. **Shifted bases** (suites 1–2, 40 runs): `brake_margin` 14 → 74.424 with **1 of 20 off** (with a further `brake_margin` −2: 1.123); `line_offset` 0.5 → 74.453, max 0.931, 0 of 20 off; **1 of 40 off** (v0.83 0 of 40; within the bar of ≤ 1). **Patterns** (`tools/patterns.py`), v0.83 → v0.84: steering reversals 67 → 73, episodes 3 → 1 (1.2 → 0.5 s; the one left is 1,456–1,480 m under braking); target flips 106 → 108; allowed-speed jumps > 20 km/h 95 → **107** (worse: `speedY` is a noisy input to the plan); brake applications 38 → 36 (short touches 19 → 17, re-applied within 10 steps 11 → **15**); 39 shifts, 0 undone; TC cut 10.8 → 10.5 s. **Width** (`tools/width.py`; entry −80 m / apex / exit +80 m), v0.83 → v0.84: 450 m +0.21/+0.25/+0.40 → +0.20/+0.17/+0.28; 786 m +0.19/−0.09/+0.34 → +0.18/−0.06/+0.19; 1,041 m +0.40/−0.45/+0.17 → +0.32/−0.48/+0.18; 1,530 m +0.09/−0.44/+0.00 → +0.09/−0.44/−0.06; 1,928 m +0.25/−0.13/+0.10 → +0.30/−0.17/+0.08; flick −0.37/+0.73/−0.06 → −0.39/+0.72/−0.06; 2,983 m +0.14/−0.43/+0.03 → +0.15/−0.43/+0.04; hairpin +0.22/+0.62/+0.45 → +0.21/+0.62/+0.46: the line is not wider (exits at 450 m and 786 m are 0.12–0.15 tighter). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — all-30 74.545 → 74.476 (−0.069 s), 0 of 30 off, suite maximum 0.851 → 0.853, new best single lap 74.378 (1:14.37). Cost: shifted bases 0 → 1 of 40 off (the `brake_margin` 14 base with a further −2, flick/hairpin margin; within the bar), and the plan flickers more (allowed-speed jumps 95 → 107). |
| **Learned** | The medium bends are grip-limited, not sight-limited (2.0–2.7 g at the slowest points), so the plan gains nothing from more or less entry speed; what it lacked was the direction of travel. Referring the sharpness geometry to that direction is worth ~0.04 s alone on v0.82 and ~0.07 s on top of the slip-ratio traction control: the two add up and a little more (less wheelspin slide on the exits means less slide for the plan to punish). High `slip_ref` with a matching `turn_grip` (≥ 0.45 / 8.8) sits next to a cliff. The plan now reads `speedY` raw: a low-passed slip angle is the obvious follow-up against the extra plan jumps (not tested). The remaining time in the medium bends is in the line radius and the exit, not the plan. Parallel workflow: a third mechanism that is real alone (upshift clutch, −0.02) can be flat on top of two others; each stacking step has to be measured on the committed base. |

| **Learned** | The flick is flat against the speed plan: capping the arrival at the kink or at the arc by 10–60 km/h moves the suite mean by ±0.04 s, and braking harder inside the arc loses 0.3 s and margin, because the arc is steering-limited at full lock. So neither the steering block (round 1) nor the plan on the approach (round 2) is the flick's lever. The braking plan's remaining time was in the high-speed cap: the clutch car arrives faster and `brake_max` 30 is now safe on the suites (1 off on v0.77). **The batch is now margin-limited, not idea-limited:** three independent gains were found on v0.84 (brake cap −0.138, apex re-tune −0.063, throttle floor −0.049; all three together 74.220, unperturbed lap 74.029), but every pair fails a safety check at the same two runs (`turn_grip_aero` + and `brake_margin` 14 with a further −2, flick and hairpin exit). A change that buys margin at those two places would unlock ~0.12 s that is already measured. From the line agent: the 450 m and 786 m bends are grip-limited over the whole arc (the car never gets inside of +0.14 / −0.21 against a ±0.81 target with the pull saturated), and both run a ~1 s limit cycle (throttle ramp → slide and yaw → steering collapses to ~0 → over the plan → brake touch) that `tools/patterns.py` does not flag. From the pedals agent: engine braking is a large part of the car's braking (declutched under braking: 10 of 10 off). |

## v0.86 — Braking downshift behind the plan (`brake_ds_rpm` 17,500, `brake_ds_over` 20)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.86 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.86`). One mechanism in the gear block: while the car brakes and is more than `brake_ds_over` **20** km/h above the allowed speed, the downshift comes as soon as the lower gear would land below `brake_ds_rpm` **17,500** instead of `downshift_rpm` 15,000 (new knobs, lines 572–573; comment at 998–1010, condition at line 1018). Developed on a private copy of v0.85 by one sequential agent and applied by the orchestrator. |
| **Why** | **The batch was margin-limited:** two measured gains (throttle floor T, apex re-tune X; v0.85 entry) could not be stacked on v0.85 because the same two perturbed runs left the track: the flick's left arc (2,477–2,482 m) with `turn_grip_aero` +0.5e-4, and the hairpin exit (~3,293 m) on the `brake_margin` 14 base with a further −2. Orchestrator's screen of known margin knobs on v0.85 + X + T, all still 1–2 of 30 off and 2–3 of 20 off on `brake_margin` 14: `corner_speed` 77, `turn_grip_aero` 1.4e-4, `slip_ref` 0.4, `lock_tte_near` 1.1, `thr_zero` 0.7. **Agent's own analysis** (step traces of the failing runs against the same runs of v0.85). Flick, `turn_grip_aero` +: the plan collapses 250 → 203 km/h in one step at 2,366 m with the car at 243; from there to 2,436 m the pedal demand is saturated and ABS-cut on every step (brake 0.500 for 70 m), the car 20–50 km/h over the plan, and it reaches the left arc at 121 km/h (2,432 m). Unperturbed v0.85 enters the kink at 232 km/h only because two brake touches from plan flicker at 2,334 and 2,346 m slow it; with `turn_grip_aero` + they do not happen (242 km/h). So the lever is deceleration the pedal cannot ask for: engine braking on the rear wheels (simuv2 `engine.cpp`: off the throttle the engine brakes by k/(1 + k) of its torque, k = 0.33·(rpm − 5,000)/15,000, times the gear ratio). With the mechanism the downshifts come at 2,366 m (5→4) and 2,378 m (4→3) instead of 2,375 and 2,405 m, the rpm under braking is 16,500–18,500 instead of 12,300–15,400, and the arc entry on the failing run is 4 km/h slower (121.1 → 117.1 km/h at 2,432 m; max \|trackPos\| 1.623 off → 0.819 on the X + T stack). **Inherited conclusions challenged:** (1) v0.85's "the flick is flat against the speed plan, arc-entry speed buys ~0.03 of margin": on the failing runs 4–5 km/h less at arc entry moves the maximum from 1.62–1.80 (off) to 0.82–0.83; the earlier diagnostic was read on suite maxima set elsewhere; (2) "arriving slower helps the hairpin": a lower end speed and brake feed-forward both made the `brake_margin` 12 run worse (the cause there is braking at full lock, brake 0.25 at steer 1.0). **Alternatives (agent, ~1,330 races; watch runs = `turn_grip_aero` + / `brake_margin` 14 with `turn_grip_aero` + / `brake_margin` 12, on v0.85 + X + T unless noted):** braking downshift **ungated** (any braking) at 17,500 / 18,000 / 18,400 → flick fixed (0.67–0.80) but all-30 on v0.85 74.498 / 74.516 / 74.504 and on the full stack 74.364 / 74.463 / 74.498 (every braking zone ends a gear too low); gate 8 / 14 / 20 / 30 km/h (suite 1) → 74.341 / 74.354 / 74.289 / 74.265; gate 25 / 30 on all 30 → 74.237 / 74.239 but the shifted flick run 1.194 off / 0.975–0.999; 18,400 at gate 20 → 74.292; new **separate end speed for the road-ahead plan** 74 / 70 / 66 / 62 km/h → flick still off, hairpin 1.35 → 1.86; new **brake feed-forward** of the planned deceleration, share 0.3 / 0.6 / 1.0 → flick off, hairpin 1.30 / 1.48 / 1.64; new **rise limit on the allowed speed** 20 / 8 / 3 km/h per step → flick off; `brake_gain` 0.06 / 0.08 / 0.10 → hairpin run on track (0.886 / 0.828 / 0.835), shifted flick run still off, unperturbed lap +0.14 to +0.40 s; `abs_cut` 0.4 / 0.6 / 0.7 and `abs_ratio` 0.75 / 0.85 → off; `downshift_rpm` 14,000 / 16,000 / 17,000 → partial (17,000: 0.919 and 1.18 off). **Unlock measured on top of this version (all-30, each 0 of 30 off and shifted 1 of 40 off):** + T 74.307; + X 74.311 (max 0.913; steering reversals 82 → 103); + X + T 74.243 (max 0.905). |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (`tools/suite.py` on the final code, orchestrator): all-30 74.347 (+0.009 s vs v0.85), 0 of 30 off, max 0.771/0.905/0.731; shifted bases 1 of 40 off. |

That speed is what the lap's margin hangs on. It has to be gated on being behind the plan: used in every braking zone it costs 0.16 s. The hairpin's shifted-base failure is a different thing (braking at full lock) and is still open; `brake_gain` 0.06 put that run back on the track in a watch-run screen (untested on the suites). ## v0.87 — Throttle floor at the engine's zero-torque point (`thr_zero` 1.0)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.87 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.87`). One mechanism in the throttle block, the **throttle floor** (new; knob at line 603, code at lines 837–846): while the car is under the allowed speed, the stored throttle is at least `thr_zero` **1.0** × the engine's zero-torque throttle k/(1 + k), k = 0.33·(rpm − 5,000)/15,000 (0.13 at 12,000 rpm, 0.21 at 17,000; `S['rpm']` as read, ~4.7 % high). No other value changed. Found by a parallel agent (γ2) on v0.84, applied on v0.86 by the orchestrator. |
| **Why** | From the simulator source (simuv2 `engine.cpp`): engine torque = Tmax·(throttle·(1 + k) − k), so below throttle k/(1 + k) the engine brakes the rear wheels. After every brake application the stored throttle restarted from 0 at +0.05 per step, so the first 3–5 steps (~0.1 s) of each restart were still engine braking while the plan already asked for speed (v0.84 traces: 2,599–2,612 m, after a 4-step brake at 190 km/h the speed keeps falling 185.5 → 183.4 km/h for 6 steps with 15–20 km/h of headroom; 418–424 m and 436–441 m in the 448 m sawtooth; 158–190 m, a 2-step plan flicker at 221 km/h costs 6 km/h and 30 steps of ramp). With `thr_zero` 1.0 nothing is tuned: the value is the simulator's. On v0.84 it was −0.049 s (all-30 74.476 → 74.427, shifted 0 of 40 off) but unsafe on v0.85 (1 of 30 off at the flick with `turn_grip_aero` +); v0.86's braking downshift gives the flick the margin. **Inherited conclusion challenged (agent γ2):** "proportional / maintenance throttle and deficit-scaled ramps all lost, so the ramp is fine": the +0.05 rate is at its limit (any faster rise puts one perturbed run off), but its starting point, 0, was inside the engine-braking range. **Alternatives (agent γ2 on v0.84, ~700 races; all-30 base 74.476, suite 1 base 74.414):** `thr_zero` 1.3 → 74.409 (max 0.874; shifted 0 of 40, max 0.996): within noise of 1.0 and nearer the cliff; 1.6 → 74.400 with 1 of 30 off; constant floor 0.15 / 0.20 / 0.25 → 74.453 / 74.402 (shifted 2 of 40 off) / 74.410; 0.30 / 0.35 / 0.45 / 0.60 → suite 1 74.389 / 74.374 (1 off) / 74.368 / 74.399 (1 off); headroom-scaled faster rise +0.05 / +0.10 / +0.20 per step → suite 1 74.313 / 74.297 / 74.327, each 1 of 10 off; upshift-step clutch alone → suite 1 74.380; with the floor → all-30 74.401 but shifted 2 of 40 off; downshift on the driven wheels' rpm → suite 1 74.461; clutch pressed while braking, pedal 1.0 → 10 of 10 off, 0.5 / 0.2 → 74.427 / 74.395. **Alternative for this slot on v0.86 (all-30, base 74.347):** the apex re-tune X (`apex_steer` 0.3 → 0.5, `line_apex` 0.34 → 0.45) → 74.311, 0 of 30 off, max 0.799 / 0.913 / 0.723, shifted 1 of 40 off (on-track max 0.970): the same time, but steering reversals 82 → 103 with a 1.4 s episode of 45 reversals at 483–525 m, so the floor went first; X and the floor together → **74.243**, 0 of 30 off, max 0.819 / 0.905 / 0.731, shifted 1 of 40 off (on-track max 0.956): measured and ready as the next version (one mechanism per version). |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (`tools/suite.py` on the final code, orchestrator): all-30 74.307 (−0.040 s vs v0.86), 0 of 30 off, max 0.790/0.898/0.730; shifted bases 1 of 40 off. |
| **Lap time** | 1:14.24 |
| **Damage** | 0 |
| **Top speed** | 284 km/h |
| **Min speed** | 58 km/h |
| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261003_112300.csv`, `tools/finalize.py`); TORCS time **74.242 s** → on screen 1:14:24 (v0.86 74.242: the unperturbed lap did not move; section changes all within ±0.022 s; best single lap stays v0.85's 74.232). Damage 0, top 284 km/h, slowest corner 58 km/h at ~3,277 m (hairpin). Max \|trackPos\| 0.717 at ~2,473 m (flick), hairpin 0.64; slides: flick 16.8 km/h (exit 7.0), kink 10.5, hairpin exit 8.4, elsewhere 21.9 at ~3,010 m. **Suites:** 74.240 / 74.328 / 74.354, **all-30 74.307** (v0.86 74.347; v0.85 74.338), max 0.790 / 0.898 / 0.730, **0 of 30 off**. **Shifted bases** (suites 1–2, 40 runs): `brake_margin` 14 → 74.303 with 1 of 20 off (further `brake_margin` −2, hairpin exit: 1.257); `line_offset` 0.5 → 74.311, max 0.957, 0 of 20 off; **1 of 40 off** (the same run as v0.84–v0.86), on-track maximum 0.957. **Patterns** (`tools/patterns.py`), v0.86 → v0.87: steering reversals 82 → 78, episodes 4 → 2 (1.8 → 1.3 s; left: 1,458–1,481 m under braking and 1,523–1,555 m, 15 small reversals in the bend); target flips 110 → 106; allowed-speed jumps > 20 km/h 104 → 105; brake applications 38 → 37 (short touches 19 → 19, re-applied within 10 steps 14 → 13); 39 shifts, 0 undone; TC cut 10.0 → 10.7 s. **Width** (`tools/width.py`; entry −80 m / apex / exit +80 m): 448 m +0.21/+0.17/+0.36; 782 m +0.19/−0.09/+0.23; 1,040 m +0.34/−0.46/+0.18; 1,529 m +0.09/−0.42/−0.08; 1,928 m +0.30/−0.16/+0.08; flick −0.37/+0.71/−0.07; 2,984 m +0.14/−0.42/+0.05; hairpin +0.20/+0.64/+0.47: the line is unchanged (448 m exit +0.28 → +0.36). |

The gain shows in the suite means, not in the unperturbed lap. Engine braking is a large part of the car's braking (declutched under braking: 10 of 10 off), which v0.86 uses on the flick approach. With v0.86's margin the apex re-tune stacks on top (74.243 measured, 0 of 30 off, 1 of 40 shifted) and is the first candidate of the next batch; the upshift-step clutch is worth another 0.03–0.07 s on top of a floor but failed the shifted bases on v0.84 and should be re-measured on this car. Batch 9 in all: 74.619 → 74.307 over 30 perturbed laps (−0.312 s), best lap 74.454 → 74.232. |

## v0.88 — Apex re-tune on a low-passed steering fade (`apex_steer` 0.5, `line_apex` 0.45, `apex_lp` 0.9)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.88 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.88`). One mechanism in the steering block, the **apex extra** of the racing line (knobs at lines 548–551, code at lines 684 and 693–699): `apex_steer` 0.3 → **0.5**, `line_apex` 0.34 → **0.45**, and the fade of the extra now reads a **low-passed \|steer\|** (`c.apex_sf`, new knob `apex_lp` **0.9** per step, ~0.2 s) instead of the previous step's raw steer. `apex_steer_fade` 0.25 unchanged (no extra from 0.75 instead of 0.55). No other value changed. |
| **Why** | Own analysis of the v0.87 lap first: in the 450 m bend the car runs at \|steer\| 0.45–0.60 for 100 m, so the apex extra (full below 0.3, none from 0.55) was mostly faded out exactly where it is meant to act (line target 0.47–0.56 instead of 0.81), and the car drifts from +0.12 to −0.45 (outside half) at 100–112 km/h; the same at 786 m (target −0.5 at steer −0.5). The plan's re-tune (X: 0.5 / 0.45) moves the fade above that range. Its cost was found to be a loop of one step: more steer → smaller inside target → less steer, so with the fade's edge at 0.5 the target and the steering swapped every step (46 reversals in 1.28 s at 486–525 m). Reading the fade from a low-passed \|steer\| breaks that loop and keeps the gain. **Inherited conclusion challenged:** "`apex_steer` 0.4 / a wider fade is slower" (batch 4: 0.4 → 76.794; batch 9 on v0.84: 0.4/0.55 → 74.639): on v0.87 the wide-fade neighbours are as fast as X (below), so that no longer holds; and "the extra must fade with steering" was tested against a speed gate (own new idea) and holds. **Candidates, all on v0.87, all-30 (base 74.307), all 0 of 30 off:** plan X (`apex_steer` 0.5, `line_apex` 0.45) → 74.244 (max 0.819/0.905/0.731; reproduces the orchestrator's number), patterns 104 reversals, 2 episodes 1.8 s; **chosen: X with the low-passed fade → 74.224**, 46 reversals, 0 episodes; its neighbours: `apex_lp` 0.8 / 0.95 → 74.245 / 74.291, `apex_steer` 0.45 / 0.55 → 74.219 (max 0.946) / 74.271, `line_apex` 0.40 / 0.50 → 74.269 / 74.276, with `apex_steer` 0.4 and fade 0.4 → 74.259. Knob-only neighbours on the raw steer (`apex_steer` / `apex_steer_fade` / `line_apex`): 0.4/0.25/0.45 → 74.280; 0.5/0.4/0.45 → 74.256; **0.4/0.4/0.45 → 74.197** (max 0.922; shifted 1 of 40 off, hairpin exit 1.295, on-track max 0.962; 60 reversals, 1 episode 0.3 s), but its own neighbours are 0.35/0.4 → 74.239, 0.45/0.4 → 74.274, 0.4/0.5 → 74.234 (shifted 1 of 40 off, 1.265), 0.3/0.5 → 74.300, 0.4/0.4 with `line_apex` 0.40 / 0.50 → 74.277 / 74.325: the whole plateau is 74.20–74.29, so 74.197 is the top of the noise, and the low-passed fade was preferred for removing the cause of the oscillation (0 episodes). **Own new idea, lost:** apex extra gated by speed instead of steering (full above 95 km/h, none below 75; no loop through the steering): `line_apex` 0.45 → 74.395 (max 0.931), 0.34 → 74.365, gate 65–80 km/h → 74.408: slower than the base, so the steering fade is doing useful work inside the medium bends, not only keeping the hairpin and flick out. ~860 races. |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (`tools/suite.py` on the final code): all-30 74.224 (−0.083 s vs v0.87), 0 of 30 off, max 0.819/0.906/0.721; shifted bases 1 of 40 off. |

| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — all-30 74.307 → 74.224 (−0.083 s), 0 of 30 off, shifted bases 1 of 40 off (same run), best single lap 1:14.04 (was v0.85's 1:14.23), steering oscillation gone (0 episodes). Costs: shifted on-track maximum 0.957 → 0.970, and more pedal activity (plan jumps +8, short brake touches +5). |
| **Learned** | The apex extra was switched off by its own steering fade in the two bends it was built for; moving the fade to 0.5–0.75 puts the apexes 0.03–0.08 further inside (776 m −0.09 → −0.17), not more: the medium bends stay grip-limited and 447 m is still on the outside half at its slowest point (+0.13). A line target that depends on the previous step's raw steer is a one-step loop and oscillates when the bend's steady steer sits on the fade's edge; a 0.2 s low-pass removes it at no cost in time (the inside integral's `line_isteer` fade and the lock throttle read the raw steer the same way: candidates). The knob surface around the re-tune is flat within noise (74.20–74.29 for 14 settings), so the gain is ~0.05–0.08 s and further apex-knob tuning is not worth races. The extra pedal activity (brake touches 19 → 24, plan jumps 105 → 113) is the mid-bend limit cycle working on a tighter line: that is the next version's target. A speed gate instead of the steering fade is slower (74.365–74.408). |

## v0.89 — Plan-riding throttle (`ride_band` 3, `ride_min` 0.2): rejected

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.89 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.89`; code restored to v0.88 afterwards). One mechanism in the throttle block: the **plan-riding throttle**. The +0.05 per step throttle ramp is scaled by the headroom under the allowed speed, `clip((allowed − speed)/ride_band, ride_min, 1)` (new knobs `ride_band` **3** km/h, `ride_min` **0.2**, lines 605–606; code at lines 843–851), so the stored throttle levels off as the car closes on the plan instead of carrying it through the plan. No other value changed. |
| **Why** | The plan's item for this version: the ~1 s limit cycle in the 450 m and 786 m bends, thought to be triggered by the pedals. Own analysis of the v0.88 step traces first (400–530 m, 4.26 s; 740–800 m, 1.67 s). Three things happen there, and only one of them is in the pedals. (1) **Speed control is bang-bang around the plan**: the stored throttle ramps 0.1 → 0.6–0.9 in 0.2–0.4 s, the speed crosses the plan, then a lift (band only 0.4 km/h at 107 km/h) or a 0.02–0.14 brake touch; 7 brake applications and 9 lifts in 400–530 m. (2) **The steering collapses are not caused by the pedals**: 394–407 m is the turn-in (target jumps to +0.92, steer 0.01 → 0.64 in two steps while braking 0.4–0.9, the car yaws 11.6° into the bend, the heading term takes the steer back to 0.01); 414–427 m is a rear slide (18 km/h, rear wheels 4–9 m/s slower than the fronts = engine braking after the 3 → 2 downshift at 406 m, in the middle of the turn-in; the same downshift comes mid turn-in at 749 m, 1,914 m and 3,239 m); 469–478 m (steer 0.68 → 0.27, angle −0.6° → −11.5° with the slide unchanged at 8–9 km/h) reads as the track opening between the bend's two arcs while the car keeps its yaw rate, not a slide (inferred from the trace: the slide does not grow while the angle does). (3) The plan itself is not monotonic into the slowest point (allowed 113 → 117 → 105 km/h at 418–436 m), so the car accelerates and then brakes 8 km/h at steer 0.6. **Inherited conclusion challenged:** "the trigger of the limit cycle is in the pedals (throttle ramp + mid-bend brake touch)": not supported. This version more than halves the brake applications in the 450 m bend (7 → 3) and the three steering collapses stay as they were; every calmer pedal variant is slower. **Candidates, all on v0.88, all-30 (base 74.224, max 0.906), all 0 of 30 off unless noted:** **plan-riding throttle (own, recorded here)** `ride_band` 3 → **74.292** (max 0.925); 6 → 74.319; 1.5 → 74.306; 3 with `ride_min` 0.5 → 74.285. **Coasting instead of engine braking in the lift band (own, new)**: throttle sent = the engine's zero-torque throttle while lifting → 74.292; the same during brake touches → 74.328; both → 74.389 (so the engine braking during lifts is not the cost). **Low-passed \|steer\| in the inside integral's fade** (the v0.88 agent's suggestion): 0.9 → 74.780; 0.5 → 74.288 (the raw steer is right for the integral). **Gear-specific 3 → 2 downshift threshold (own, new; the downshift lands in the middle of the turn-in)**: later, 14,000 / 13,000 / 12,000 / 11,000 / 10,000 / 9,000 → 74.327 / 74.341 (1 off) / 74.411 (1 off) / 74.457 (1 off) / 74.520 (2 off) / 74.548 (5 off), off at the hairpin exit (1.14–1.87, the `brake_margin` −2 run first): second gear's engine braking is part of the hairpin's braking; earlier, 15,500 / 16,000 / 16,500 / 17,000 / 17,500 → 74.265 / 74.322 / 74.295 / 74.342 / 74.353 (unperturbed flick 0.69–0.71, no time). **Pedal knobs re-scanned on this car:** `touch_keep` 0.5 / 0.85 → 74.271 / 74.220; `touch_brake` 0.10 / 0.22 → 74.228 / 74.222 (max 0.926); `lift_pct` 0.6 / 1.4 → 74.275 / 74.224 (max 0.980); `lift_vw` 30 → 74.247; `lift_v0` 60 / 100 → 74.233 / 74.269; `thr_zero` 1.3 → 74.262. Nothing beats 74.224 by more than noise (best 74.220). ~1,070 races. |

Damage 0, top 284 km/h, slowest corner 57 km/h at ~3,278 m (hairpin). Max \|trackPos\| 0.721 at ~2,476 m (flick), hairpin 0.67; slides: flick 15.4 km/h (exit 6.4), kink 9.7, hairpin exit 6.6, elsewhere 20.8 at ~3,007 m. **Suites:** 74.286 / 74.316 / 74.274, **all-30 74.292** (v0.88 74.224), max 0.818 / 0.925 / 0.728, **0 of 30 off**. **Shifted bases** (suites 1–2, 40 runs): `brake_margin` 14 → 74.313 with 1 of 20 off (further `brake_margin` −2, hairpin exit: 1.272 at ~3,292 m); `line_offset` 0.5 → 74.352, max 0.963, 0 of 20 off; **1 of 40 off** (the same run as v0.84–v0.88), on-track maximum 0.967. **Limit cycle, v0.88 → v0.89:** 400–530 m: 4.26 → 4.30 s, brake applications 7 → 3, lifts 9 → 7, steering collapses (\|steer\| from over 0.4 to under 0.3) 3 → 3, largest slide 18.1 → 18.2 km/h, mean speed under the plan 0.3 → 2.0 km/h; 740–800 m: 1.67 → 1.69 s, brake applications 2 → 2, collapses 1 → 1, slide 14.3 → 18.7 km/h. **Patterns** (`tools/patterns.py`), v0.88 → v0.89: steering reversals 46 → 50, episodes 0 → 0; target flips 109 → 107; allowed-speed jumps > 20 km/h 113 → 106; brake applications 42 → 37 (short touches 24 → 20, re-applied within 10 steps 17 → 13); shifts 39 → 41, 1 undone within 1 s (was 0); TC cut 10.1 → 10.2 s. **Width** (`tools/width.py`; entry −80 m / apex / exit +80 m): 450 m +0.20/+0.20/+0.31 (was +0.22/+0.13/+0.32: further outside at the slowest point); 776 m +0.21/−0.21/+0.24 (was +0.20/−0.17/+0.29; 118 → 120 km/h); 1,043 m +0.33/−0.49/+0.18 (entry was +0.36); 1,522 m +0.09/−0.45/−0.11 (was +0.10/−0.37/−0.14; 135 → 139 km/h); 1,929 m +0.31/−0.20/+0.05 (unchanged within 0.01); flick −0.39/+0.71/−0.06 (unchanged within 0.01); 2,985 m +0.14/−0.47/+0.17 (exit was +0.02); hairpin +0.08/+0.67/+0.46 (entry was +0.22; 58 → 57 km/h). |
| **Decision** | ❌ Rejected — all-30 74.224 → 74.292 (+0.068 s), lap 1:14.04 → 1:14.22; safe (0 of 30 off, shifted bases 1 of 40 off, the same run) but slower, and not an enabling change: it removes brake touches (42 → 37 a lap, 7 → 3 in the 450 m bend) without removing the steering collapses or the slide, and no follow-up that recovers the time was found. Code restored to v0.88. |
| **Learned** | The "limit cycle" of the 450 m / 786 m bends is three separate things, and the pedals are the smallest: the bang-bang speed control around the plan costs nothing measurable (five calmer forms are 0.01–0.17 s slower: a levelled-off ramp, coasting in lifts, coasting in touches, both, a lift band from a lower speed). The steering collapses come from the turn-in (a 0.6 steer step under braking, 11° of yaw), from what reads as the track opening between the two arcs of the 450 m bend, and from a rear slide that follows the 3 → 2 downshift in the middle of the turn-in; moving that downshift later is slower and runs wide at the hairpin (second gear's engine braking is part of the braking there), moving it earlier is flat. The pedal knobs (`touch_keep`, `touch_brake`, `lift_pct`, `lift_v0`, `lift_vw`, `thr_zero`) are flat within noise on the v0.88 car (74.22–74.28). The inside integral must keep reading the raw steer (low-passed 0.9: +0.56 s). What is left in these bends is the plan and the turn-in: the allowed speed rises and then falls 12 km/h into the slowest point of the 450 m bend (418–436 m), and the car reaches the slowest point on the outside half (+0.13 to +0.20) after the turn-in overshoot. |

## v0.90 — First gear at full lock (`lock_gear_on` 0.96, `lock_gear_v` 105, `lock_gear_off` 0.72)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.90 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.90`). One mechanism in the gear block: **first gear at full lock**. In 2nd gear, with \|steer\| above `lock_gear_on` (**0.96**) and the speed below `lock_gear_v` (**105** km/h), the car shifts down to 1st; it shifts back up to 2nd once \|steer\| falls below `lock_gear_off` (**0.72**). Not during the launch. New knobs at lines 577–579, code at lines 1040–1051 (before the `c.up_t` line); the `lowest_running_gear` comment (line 576) now names the exception. No other value changed. |
| **Why** | The plan's item: margin at the hairpin exit, where the one shifted run (`brake_margin` 14 with the suite's −2 = 12) has left the track since v0.84. Own analysis first, of the v0.88 run and of that failing run step by step (3,150–3,330 m). **What makes it leave the track:** not the braking at full lock as such, but the speed the car arrives with. Both cars start the full-lock arc at the same place and position (3,254 m, `trackPos` +0.13 / +0.14), the failing one at 104 km/h instead of 97; it then drifts out at ~1 `trackPos` unit per second with the front tyres saturated, off the throttle and (from 3,266 m) off the brake, and crosses the edge at 3,277 m still doing 60 km/h. The hairpin exit position follows the brake margin smoothly: \|trackPos\| 0.66 / 0.76 / 0.91 / **1.29** / 1.76 at `brake_margin` 15 / 14 / 13 / 12 / 11 (a "margin ladder", used as the screen for every candidate, with the unperturbed lap and the flick run `turn_grip_aero` + alongside). The approach is tyre-limited all the way (pedal 0.72–0.75, ABS cutting, ~30 m/s²), so the speed cannot be taken off earlier without braking earlier, which costs lap time. **Inherited conclusion challenged:** "the run fails because of braking at full lock (brake 0.25 at steer 1.0)". Measured with a brake multiplier at full lock: no brake there → the ladder gets worse (unperturbed hairpin 0.89, `brake_margin` 14 already off at 1.10), 1.5× / 2× brake there → worse too (`brake_margin` 12: 1.69 / 1.90). The pedal at lock is near its best; the cause is the arrival speed. **Candidates (ladder = hairpin \|trackPos\| at `brake_margin` 15 / 14 / 13 / 12, base 0.66 / 0.76 / 0.91 / 1.29):** (1) friction-circle brake release at lock (plan's idea): above, lost. (2) **Edge-time brake** (new: brake at lock when the time to the outside edge is short): 0.5 pedal 0.64 / 0.73 / 0.88 / 1.13, 0.3 pedal from 0.7 s 0.65 / 0.75 / 0.90 / 0.99, 1.0 pedal off from `brake_margin` 13: a little margin, not enough. (3) **Late turn-in** (new: inside pull held off while the car is over the plan): 10 km/h over → lap 75.70, `brake_margin` 12 0.97 but 11 at 1.67; 5 / 0 km/h → 76.8–76.9 and off from `brake_margin` 14: the early move to the inside is needed (also answers the v0.89 turn-in overshoot idea: removing it is slower). (4) 3 → 2 downshift earlier (plan's idea, `ds32` 16,000 / 17,500): `brake_margin` 12 still 1.25 / 1.38. (5) **First gear at full lock** (new, gated by time to the edge 1.2 s / 2.0 s, or ungated): 0.60 / 0.72 / 0.85 / **0.95**, 0.60 / 0.70 / 0.82 / 0.94, **0.57 / 0.65 / 0.79 / 0.92** — the whole ladder moves by about one metre of brake margin, at an unchanged lap (74.03). Knob screen on the ladder (`brake_margin` 12 value): `line_gain` 0.6 / 0.7 1.38 / 1.55, `line_offset` 0.55 1.51, `setup_pull` 0 / 0.25 1.60 / **0.98**, `max_steer_step` 0.3 1.34, `turn_steer_max` 0.70 / 0.85 1.35 / 1.27, `fade_lp` 0.8 1.22, `brake_ds_over` 10 / 14 1.21 / 1.23, `abs_ratio` 0.85 1.90, `abs_cut` 0.6 1.50, `lookahead_gain` 2.4 1.27, `line_imax` 0.6 1.16, `line_isteer` 0.8 1.43, `lock_steer` 0.7 1.28. `setup_pull` 0.2 / 0.25 / 0.3 on the suites: all-30 74.361 / 74.285 / 74.323 (slower), max 0.947 / 0.962 / 0.938, shifted bases still 1 of 40 off (another run): chaos, not margin. **Chosen: (5), ungated** (the gate bought nothing: all-30 74.196 / 74.169 / 74.178 at 1.2 s / 2.0 s / none, shifted on-track max 0.952 / 0.938 / 0.921; fewer knobs). Its three knobs are on a plateau (all-30, base 74.178): `lock_gear_on` 0.9 / 0.8 → 74.161 / 74.161, `lock_gear_off` 0.5 / 0.9 → 74.166 / 74.155, `lock_gear_v` 125 / 90 → 74.163 / 74.143 (90: max 0.841, `brake_margin` 14 base max 0.973: spends the margin). Earlier results it bears on: `lowest_running_gear` 1 (1st everywhere below 2nd's range) was slower (v0.23; batch 9: suite 1 74.681) because the exits were driven in 1st; here 1st is held only while the wheel is at full lock. |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (`tools/suite.py` on the final code): all-30 74.178 (−0.046 s vs v0.88), 0 of 30 off, max 0.774/0.791/0.732; shifted bases 0 of 40 off, on-track max 0.921. |

| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261003_133612.csv`, `tools/finalize.py`); TORCS time **74.030 s** → on screen 1:14:03 (v0.88 74.042: −0.012 s). Damage 0, top 284 km/h, slowest corner 53 km/h at ~3,273 m (hairpin; 58 on v0.88). Max \|trackPos\| 0.714 at ~2,473 m (flick), hairpin 0.57 (v0.88 0.66); slides: flick 18.6 km/h (exit 10.4), kink 9.8, hairpin exit 8.4, elsewhere 24.3 at ~3,272 m (the hairpin's rotation, see Learned). **Suites:** 74.214 / 74.201 / 74.118, **all-30 74.178** (v0.88 74.224), max 0.774 / 0.791 / 0.732 (v0.88 0.819 / 0.906 / 0.721), **0 of 30 off**. **Shifted bases: 0 of 40 off** (`brake_margin` 14: 74.112 / 74.149, max 0.772 / 0.921, the 0.921 being the old failing run, `brake_margin` −2, at the hairpin exit; `line_offset` 0.5: 74.187 / 74.287, max 0.782 / 0.796): the first 0 of 40 since v0.83. 1st gear is used five times a lap: 2,440–2,445 m and 2,451–2,473 m (flick left arc, 97 → 62 km/h), 2,487–2,499 m and 2,506–2,513 m (flick right arc), 3,255–3,271 m (hairpin, 96 → 56 km/h); highest engine reading in 1st there 15,200 rpm. **Patterns** (`tools/patterns.py`, v0.88 → v0.90): steering reversals 46 → 42, episodes 0 → 0; target flips 109 → 105; plan jumps 113 → 112; brake applications 42 → 41 (short touches 24 → 23, re-applied within 10 steps 17 → 17); shifts 39 → **49, 7 undone within 1 s** (0 before: these are the mechanism's own 2 → 1 → 2 stints of 0.2–1.3 s, not hunting between thresholds; the 0.2 s one at 2,440–2,445 m is a steering dip to 0.65 on the flick's left arc); TC cut time 10.1 → 11.0 s. **Width** (`tools/width.py`, entry −80 m / slowest point / exit +80 m): all corners to 2,986 m unchanged within 0.01; hairpin slowest point +0.66 → +0.52 of the outside (the tool reads the side as R at the new slowest point, 3,273 m, where the car counter-steers; signs converted), exit +0.47 → +0.39, exit speed 173 → 169 km/h at +80 m. **Follow-ups measured on top** (all-30; shifted bases): `brake_load_min` 0.7 → **74.109**, 0 of 30 off, max 0.958; shifted 1 of 40 off (`brake_margin` 14 with −2, 1.145); `corner_speed` 79 → 74.172, max 0.821; shifted 0 of 40, max 0.963; `brake_ds_over` 25 → 74.178, max 0.773; shifted 0 of 40, max 0.912; `thr_zero` 1.3 → 74.190, max 0.778; shifted 0 of 40, max 0.917. |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — all-30 74.224 → 74.178 (−0.046 s), lap 1:14.04 → 1:14.03, 0 damage, 0 of 30 off with the suite max down from 0.906 to 0.791, and shifted bases 1 of 40 off → **0 of 40** (on-track max 0.970 → 0.921). It meets the bar on time alone and is the margin the plan asked for at both watch points (hairpin exit and the flick's left arc). |
| **Learned** | At full lock the car is front-limited: brake pedal in either direction makes the drift to the outside worse, but braking the **rear wheels only** helps twice, by slowing the car and by turning it in. The only rear-only brake the driver has is the engine: 1st instead of 2nd is ~2 m/s² more off-throttle deceleration at 60–100 km/h. In the hairpin the rear steps out (sideways speed 9 → 24 km/h at 3,266–3,273 m), the nose comes round, the driver counter-steers (−0.6) and the car leaves the corner 0.09 further from the edge at the same lap time; the slowest point is 5 km/h lower but earlier, and the exit is straighter. The same thing on the flick's left arc is why the `turn_grip_aero` + run is no longer the suite maximum (0.906 → below 0.80). The hairpin exit position is a smooth function of the arrival speed (about 0.10–0.15 of `trackPos` per metre of brake margin, then a cliff once the car is past 0.95); the mechanism is worth about one metre of brake margin there. The margin is real but not large: `brake_load_min` 0.7 on top is 0.07 s faster and puts the old run off again (1.145), while `corner_speed` 79, `brake_ds_over` 25 and `thr_zero` 1.3 are flat with 0 of 40 off. Also learned: the early move to the inside before the hairpin is needed (holding it off is 1.7–2.9 s slower and off the track); a stronger outside set-up (`setup_pull` 0.25) rescues the failing run but only by moving the chaos (another shifted run goes off, all-30 +0.06 s). Concern for later versions: the hairpin now has a deliberate 24 km/h slide; anything that adds rear slip there (traction-control or throttle changes at lock) should be checked on the hairpin trace. |

## v0.91 — Crest load floor by speed (`brake_load_fast` 0.8, `brake_load_v0` 150, `brake_load_vw` 50)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.91 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.91`). One mechanism in the braking plan: the **crest load floor rises with speed**. The plan scales `brake_decel` by the tyre load read from the vertical acceleration, clipped between a floor and 1; the floor was `brake_load_min` 0.5 at every speed. Now it is `brake_load_min` (**0.5**, unchanged) up to `brake_load_v0` (**150** km/h) and rises linearly to `brake_load_fast` (**0.8**) over `brake_load_vw` (**50** km/h), so 0.8 from 200 km/h. New knobs at lines 542–544, code at lines 789–790 (new line 789 `load_floor=`), comment lines 773–784. No other value changed. |
| **Why** | The plan's item: spend the margin v0.90 bought, with `brake_load_min` 0.5 → 0.7 as the strongest measured candidate (74.109, but suite max 0.958 and the shifted hairpin run off again). Own analysis first, with the load logged per step on the v0.90 lap. **Where the load floor acts at all:** the load is below 0.7 only at 200–250 m (start straight, plan not binding), 2,000–2,100 m, and 2,200–2,650 m (crest at 2,218–2,260 m: load below 0.7 for 0.6 s and 0 for 0.3 s at 265 km/h; flick crest 2,367–2,386 m: load 0; flick turn-in 2,431–2,438 m: 0.36–0.69 at 110 km/h; after the Corkscrew 2,598–2,604 m: 0.28 at 190 km/h). The hairpin approach never goes below 0.91. **Where uniform 0.7 gains and costs** (unperturbed lap against v0.90): −0.08 s at 2,400–2,475 m, because the car reaches the flick's left arc faster (82 → 89 km/h at 2,450 m, flick \|trackPos\| 0.71 → 0.77), and −0.04 s at 2,600–2,625 m (no brake at 191 km/h after the Corkscrew: 201 against 190 km/h at 2,625 m). So two thirds of its gain *is* the flick margin, and one third is free. **Inherited conclusion challenged:** "`brake_load_min` 0.7 puts the hairpin run off, so it spends the hairpin margin": the hairpin approach has no crest; that run (`brake_margin` 14 − 2) sits at 0.92 on the cliff and moves by chaos from the changed lap before it. The real cost is at the flick's left arc. **Candidates (all-30, base 74.178, max 0.791; all 0 of 30 off unless noted):** (1) plan's knob, uniform floor: 0.6 → 74.166 (max 0.802; shifted 0 of 40, max 0.923), 0.65 → 74.164 (max 0.888), 0.7 → 74.109 (max 0.958; shifted 1 of 40 off, from the v0.90 entry). (2) **Floor by speed (own, new; recorded here):** low floor kept below 150 km/h (the flick turn-in), high at speed: `brake_load_fast` 0.7 / 0.75 / **0.8** / 0.85 / 0.9 → 74.114 / 74.070 / **74.086** / 74.083 / 74.109 (max 0.847 / 0.933 / **0.851** / 0.861 / 0.966); 1.0 → 2 of 30 off (1.74 and 1.70, `turn_grip_aero` + and `brake_margin` −2; location not checked), 1.0 from 180 km/h → 1 off, 1.0 from 120 km/h → 6 off: the crest protection is needed at speed too, only less of it. Ramp on a plateau at 0.8: `brake_load_v0` 130 / 170 → 74.101 / 74.084, `brake_load_vw` 25 / 80 → 74.098 / 74.112, 170 with 30 → 74.086 (max 0.848–0.851). (3) **Crest as lost braking distance (own, new):** the load takes `crest_len`·(1 − load) metres off the visible road instead of scaling the deceleration over the whole distance: 10 / 20 / 30 m → 74.276 / 74.226 / 74.290 (max 0.844 / 0.774 / 0.769); with a floor of 0: 20 / 30 / 40 m → 74.252 / 74.336 / 74.392 (max 0.779 / 0.767 / 0.786): more margin in suites 1 and 3 (0.67–0.73) but 0.05–0.21 s slower. (4) **Load smoothing (own):** share of the previous value kept per step 0.7 → 0.5 / 0.85 / 0.9 → 74.192 / 74.156 / 74.120 (max 0.768 / 0.884 / 0.819; 0.9: shifted 0 of 40, max 0.919); on top of the chosen form 0.9 → 74.075 but max 0.942: not taken. (5) **Upshift-step clutch** (plan's ready-made patch) → **74.118**, max 0.777, shifted 0 of 40 (max 0.922): a gain at unchanged margin, a different mechanism, left for the next version (stack measured, see Observed). **Chosen: (2) at 0.8 / 150 / 50**: the largest gain (−0.092 s, more than uniform 0.7's −0.069) with shifted bases 0 of 40 and the suite max at 0.851 instead of 0.958; 0.75 is 0.016 s faster but at 0.933. ~1,160 races. |

| **Observed** | Run automatically (`runs/run_20261003_135237.csv`, `tools/finalize.py`; rerun byte-identical); TORCS time **73.974 s** → on screen 1:13:97 (v0.90 74.030: −0.056 s; largest section changes 700 m +0.110, 2,400 m −0.080, 2,600 m −0.040, 3,100 m −0.026, 3,500 m −0.022, 3,300 m +0.022). Damage 0, top 284 km/h, slowest corner 53 km/h at ~3,273 m (hairpin). Max \|trackPos\| 0.679 at ~2,476 m (flick; v0.90 0.714), hairpin 0.56; slides: flick 12.8 km/h (exit 12.0), kink 14.9 (v0.90 9.8), hairpin exit 8.3, elsewhere 24.0 at ~3,272 m (the hairpin's rotation in 1st, as v0.90). **Suites:** 74.066 / 74.136 / 74.054, **all-30 74.086** (v0.90 74.178), max 0.851 / 0.784 / 0.728 (v0.90 0.774 / 0.791 / 0.732), **0 of 30 off**; the 0.851 is the `turn_grip_aero` + run on the flick's left arc (~2,461 m), next `turn_grip` +0.5 at 0.773. **Shifted bases: 0 of 40 off** (`brake_margin` 14: 74.057 / 74.060, max 0.866 / 0.919, the 0.919 being the run with a further −2 at the hairpin exit, ~3,277 m; `line_offset` 0.5: 74.162 / 74.113, max 0.781 / 0.795). **Where it acts on the lap:** 2,287–2,298 m: the brake for the crest is pedal 0.35–0.67 and 275 → 259 km/h (v0.90: pedal 1.0, 275 → 253); 2,600 m: a two-step lift at 193 km/h instead of a 0.26–0.30 brake (190 → 186); the flick's left arc is entered at 81 km/h at 2,450 m (v0.90 82), because the floor is unchanged below 150 km/h. The 700 m section is 0.11 s slower on this lap (786 m bend: slowest point 118 → 116 km/h, position −0.17 → −0.05 of the inside): the plan first differs from v0.90's at 206 m (load 0.75 at 227 km/h), the lap is a different one from the start straight on, and this bend's outcome is within the lap-to-lap spread; the suite means carry the result. **Patterns** (`tools/patterns.py`, v0.90 → v0.91): steering reversals 42 → 45, episodes 0 → 1 (0.26 s at 536–546 m: the set-up target flips side four times on the exit of the 450 m bend at 137 km/h, steering change 0.07); target flips 105 → 105; plan jumps > 20 km/h 112 → 106; brake applications 41 → 39 (short touches 23 → 22, re-applied within 10 steps 17 → 14); shifts 49 → 47, undone within 1 s 7 → 3 (the flick is now two stints in 1st, 2,446–2,472 m and 2,487–2,499 m, instead of four); TC cut time 11.0 → 11.8 s (not traced to a place). **Width** (`tools/width.py`, entry −80 m / slowest point / exit +80 m): 447 m +0.22/+0.15/+0.30 (was +0.22/+0.13/+0.32); 771 m +0.20/−0.05/+0.32 (was +0.20/−0.17/+0.29: further from the inside on this lap); 1,046 m +0.37/−0.48/+0.19 (within 0.01); 1,524 m +0.10/−0.38/−0.13 (within 0.01; 135 → 138 km/h); 1,927 m +0.30/−0.17/+0.09 (exit was +0.06); flick −0.29/+0.67/−0.08 (was −0.38/+0.71/−0.07; entry speed 193 → 199 km/h); a new row at 2,674 m (L, 146 km/h, −0.17/−0.27/−0.36: the bend after the Corkscrew is now a slowest point of its own because the car arrives faster); 2,985 m and the hairpin within 0.01. **Follow-up measured on top:** upshift-step clutch → all-30 **74.038**, 0 of 30 off, max 0.837; shifted bases 0 of 40 off (`brake_margin` 14: 73.991 / 74.051, max 0.855 / 0.921; `line_offset` 0.5: 74.117 / 74.115, max 0.825 / 0.817). |
| **Decision** | ✅ Kept — all-30 74.178 → 74.086 (−0.092 s), lap 1:14.03 → 1:13.97 (the first lap under 1:14), 0 damage, 0 of 30 off, shifted bases 0 of 40 off (on-track max 0.921 → 0.919). It spends part of the flick margin v0.90 bought (suite max 0.791 → 0.851, on the `turn_grip_aero` + run) and none at the hairpin. |
| **Learned** | The crest load factor is two things. At speed (200+ km/h, 45–100 m of road in view) it is mostly a false alarm: the crest is over long before the corner, and a floor of 0.5 cost a full brake application at 2,290 m and a brake touch after the Corkscrew; 0.8 there is free time (plateau 0.7–0.85; 1.0 leaves the track, so some protection is still needed). Close to the flick (below 150 km/h) it is the car's arc-entry speed: a higher floor there is the 0.08 s that uniform 0.7 gained and the margin it spent. Splitting the floor by speed keeps the first and leaves the second. Scaling the deceleration is also a better form than taking a fixed length off the road (0.05–0.21 s slower, though it adds margin: a candidate if margin is needed again). The hairpin approach has no crest (load ≥ 0.91): the shifted hairpin run moves only by chaos when the lap before it changes, and it stays at 0.92, close to the cliff. The upshift-step clutch stacks on this (74.038, shifted 0 of 40): ready for the next version. Open: a slower load low-pass (0.9) gains alone (74.120) but adds nothing safe on top (max 0.942). |

## v0.92 — Upshift clutch hold (`shift_steps` 3)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v0.92 |
| **What changed** | `snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v0.92`). One mechanism in the gear block: the **upshift clutch hold**. For `shift_steps` (**3**) steps from an upshift (the car's 0.05 s shift time is 2.5 steps), while accelerating (no brake, stored throttle > 0), the clutch pedal is held at `clutch_slip` (0.667, unchanged: the most at which the simulator still passes the full engine torque). New knob at line 620, code at lines 1081–1082 (after `R['gear']= gear`), comment lines 1071–1080. No other value changed. |
| **Why** | The plan's item: the ready-made upshift-step clutch (pedal on the upshift step worked out from the new gear's wheel rpm), measured by the v0.91 agent at 74.038. Own analysis first, of the v0.91 run: time by pedal state is 39.0 s at full throttle, 11.4 s with traction control cutting, 6.7 s on the throttle ramp, 3.0 s lifting, 14.6 s braking; 21 upshifts under power a lap; the largest sections are 2,400 m (3.96 s), 3,200 m (3.70 s) and 400 m (3.32 s). The simulator source (simuv2 `transmission.cpp`): after any gear change, for the shift time, it opens the clutch itself and caps the throttle at 0.1 on every simulation step where the pedal is below 0.01; the driver's pedal on the upshift step came from the old gear's wheel rpm (above `clutch_top`, so 0), and on the next steps from the new gear's (15,000–16,700 rpm, so already slipping, in every gear including 5th). Step trace on v0.91, 3rd → 4th at 126 m: speed +0.1 km/h in the shift step instead of +0.6–0.7, engine reading 19,494 → 15,788 rpm at once. **Inherited conclusion challenged:** the plan's note "holding the pedal for the whole shift time (2–3 steps) was not tried" assumed the patch covers only the first step. With the patch, a pedal floor of 0.02 for three steps on top changes nothing (all 30 runs identical): the slip law already holds the pedal on steps 2–3. And the patch's own pedal value is not what decides it either: a bare 0.02 on the shift step gives the same mean. What is left to choose is how hard and how long the clutch is slipped through the shift. **Candidates (all-30, base 74.086, max 0.851; all 0 of 30 off; paired = mean per-run difference to v0.91 over the 30 runs, runs faster, t value):** (1) plan's patch → 74.038 (suites 74.045 / 74.085 / 73.983, max 0.837; paired −0.048, 18 of 30, t −1.3; shifted bases 0 of 40, 74.021 / 74.116, max 0.921). (2) **Pedal floor on the shift step only (own):** 0.02 / 0.1 / 0.3 / 0.667 → 74.040 / 74.032 / 74.016 / 74.011 (max 0.794 / 0.814 / 0.854 / 0.796; paired −0.045 to −0.075, 20–22 of 30, t −1.8 to −3.1; 0.02: shifted 0 of 40, 73.984 / 74.025, max 0.932). (3) **Pedal held for the shift time (own; recorded here):** 0.667 for 2 / **3** / 4 / 5 / 6 / 8 / 12 steps → 74.045 / **73.964** / 74.020 / 74.014 / 73.998 / 74.030 / 74.050 (max 0.797 / 0.851 / 0.783 / 0.790 / 0.809 / 0.802 / 0.796); for 3 steps at 0.5 / 0.6 / 0.72 → 73.988 / 74.016 / 74.031 (max 0.850 / 0.801 / 0.793). Shifted bases (`brake_margin` 14 / `line_offset` 0.5 means), all 0 of 40 off: 3 steps 74.065 / 74.070 (max 0.918), 4 steps 73.994 / 74.121 (0.919), 6 steps 74.065 / 74.134 (0.895), 0.5 for 3 steps 74.068 / 74.058 (0.912). (4) **Clutch floor after downshifts (own, new: the same simulator rule opens the clutch for 0.05 s on every downshift, so the engine braking is interrupted for 2–3 steps):** floor 0.02 for 3 steps → 74.079, max 0.941 (suite 2, `brake_margin` −2); with (1) → 74.092, max 0.934: no gain and the margin is spent. (5) **Throttle ramp faster when the wheel is straight (own, new):** +0.05 more per step at steer 0, none from 0.3 → 74.076 (suites 74.137 / 74.086 / 74.005, max 0.851): flat. (6) Slip-law re-tune instead of a shift rule: `clutch_k` 30 / 15 → 74.100 / 74.139; `clutch_top` 17,500 → 74.063: no. **Chosen: (3) at 3 steps, pedal `clutch_slip`:** the largest and most consistent gain (paired −0.121, 27 of 30 runs faster, t −6.8), 3 steps is the shift time itself, one new knob, shifted bases 0 of 40 off. ~1,450 races. |
| **Prediction** | Measured directly (`tools/suite.py` on the final code): all-30 73.964 (−0.122 s vs v0.91), 0 of 30 off, max 0.851/0.796/0.725; shifted bases 0 of 40 off, on-track max 0.918. |
| **Lap time** | 1:13.82 |
