## v1.11 — Planned line (track memory): a whole-lap racing line computed offline from the track's geometry, followed by the steering inside two zones (60–2,150 m and 2,600–3,160 m), with the line's speed as a cap on the sensor plan

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Version** | v1.11 |
| **What changed** | `driver/snakeoil3_v1.py` (Git tag `v1.11`). One mechanism, set by the user (2026-10-07: "since we can memorize the track, can't we precompute the most optimal racing line and adjust that? We should use as much track as possible"): **a precomputed whole-lap racing line, stored as a table by `distFromStart`, used as the steering's target.** **Offline (new tool `tools/raceline.py`, own commit):** the segments of `C:\torcs\torcs\tracks\road\corkscrew\corkscrew.xml` are rebuilt as TORCS builds them (arcs cut into steps of equal length, radius changing linearly per step: the rebuilt lap is 3,608.45 m and closes on itself to 0.0 m / 0.00°, and its corners sit where the telemetry has them); stations every 3 m are moved along the track's normal inside a usable half-width by K1999-style curvature smoothing (close to the minimum-curvature line); a point-mass model (sideways grip 15.5·(1 + 5e-4·v²) m/s² fitted to our laps, braking 14 + 0.0065·v² up to 34 m/s² sharing the grip) gives the speed the line allows. Table written with `python tools/raceline.py --limit 0.65 --zone 990:1090:0.5 --zone 2940:3030:0.5 --table plan.txt`: 361 rows, one per 10 m. **In the driver:** new knob-block lines 680–703: `plan_zones` ((60, 2150), (2600, 3160)), `plan_ramp` 30 m, `plan_kp` 0.5, `plan_kh` 4.77, `plan_slipmax` 5°, `plan_ff` 8.0, `plan_ffv` 9e-4, `plan_la` 0.15 s, `plan_vs` 1.0, and the table `plan_ds` 10, `plan_pos` (trackPos), `plan_curv` (1/km, + = left), `plan_v` (km/h). **Steering (new code 853–876, after the sensor steering and before the steering cap and rate limit, which still apply):** inside a zone `steer = (angle − clip(atan(speedY/speedX), ±plan_slipmax) − line direction)·plan_kh + (plan_pos − trackPos)·plan_kp + plan_curv(distance + plan_la·v)·plan_ff·(1 + plan_ffv·v²)`, with the line direction = −atan(6·d plan_pos/d distance); it replaces the sensor steering (centring, look-ahead bearing, line / turn tables, set-up, apex, integral) with weight 1 inside a zone, fading over `plan_ramp` at its ends; the sensor steering is still computed every step, so its state is live at the hand-over. **Speed (new code 1041–1045, before the corner table):** inside a zone `allowed = min(sensor plan, plan_v·plan_vs)`; the corner table's offsets are added after it as before. Line mapping from v1.10: old ≤ 679 identity, old 680–828 +24, old 829–992 +48, old ≥ 993 +53 (knob block now 536–703; `prev_steer=` 680 → 704; turn-table loop 717 → 741; line-table loop 758 → 782; `c.aim, c.line_target…` 829 → 877; corner-table loop 993 → 1046). Outside the zones (the start to 60 m, 2,150–2,600 m = the flick approach, the Corkscrew and its exit, 3,160 m to the line = the hairpin and the finish straight) the driver is v1.10 unchanged. Braking plan, lift band, ABS, traction control, gears and clutch are untouched. Also: `tools/patterns.py` fix in its own commit (the TC line summed a step of −70 s when the log has a row after the finish line). **How the officials' constraint is kept:** the table only says where the line is, how it bends and what speed it can carry; every step the steering closes the loop on the live `trackPos`, `angle` and `speedY` / `speedX`, the allowed speed is still made by the beams (road ahead, sharpness plan, S-bend look, corner table) and only capped by the line's speed, and brake pedal, ABS and TC react to the live readings. No control is replayed by distance; nothing is learned on track. |
| **Why** | Assignment: fourth version of batch 14; the mechanism was set by the user. Base = v1.10's 70 kept runs (`%TEMP%\v110\final` copied to `%TEMP%\v111\base`; a variant with the plan off reproduced `runs/run_20261007_223637.csv` byte for byte). **Own analysis first:** (i) `tools/width.py` on the v1.10 lap: entries +0.20 to +0.49 of the outside, slowest points −0.03 to −0.42 of the inside (446 m and the flick on the outside half), exits −0.00 to +0.35: about half of the road is used at most corners; (ii) the geometry says the bends are short arcs on a 12 m road (1,931 m: 54° at radius 33.6 m; 770 m: 94° at 44–46 m), so a line through the full width has 2–3 times the centre line's radius there: the tool's line has radius 93 m at 1,931 m; (iii) the driven lap's sideways acceleration (hairpin 18.7 m/s² at 73 km/h, 450 m 21.6 at 102, 1,528 m 29 at 150) fits grip 15.5·(1 + 5e-4·v²), used for the line's speed. **Own candidates:** (1) the line as the target of the existing pull machinery (a generated `line_table` with in-bend targets): not run, because (2) was run first and worked; (2) **a line follower replacing the steering in zones: direction of travel against the line's direction + position error + curvature feed-forward** (chosen); (3) new, found on the way: the line's own speed as a cap on the sensor plan (part of the chosen mechanism: without it the car arrives 20 km/h over at 450 m); (4) new, tried and dropped: traction control keyed to the grip share the line's curvature needs instead of \|steer\| (the follower steers 0.2–0.4 where the old line steered 0.5–0.6): 70.879 against 70.875 on 12 runs, no difference. **Inherited conclusions challenged:** (a) v1.00's "a wide entry alone is slower at every medium bend" and "corner speed follows the plan, not the line": with a planned line and its turn-in, entries at +0.43 to +0.65 and apexes at −0.67 to −0.69 are 0.12–0.36 s faster per corner (table under Observed); (b) "the sensor turn-in step is a strong local optimum" (batch 11): true for the sensor line, the follower has no turn-in step at all; (c) "a stronger proportional pull fights the heading term at 210–260 km/h" (v1.00): the follower commands a direction (the line's) and tracks within 0.01–0.02 of the line on the straights, with 20 steering reversals on the lap against 36. **Forms tried, in order** (single unperturbed laps unless noted; base 72.542): **lap-wide, heading from the nose, no speed cap:** off at 561 m (the car arrives at 450 m at 126 km/h, 20 over; the beams look across the bend); **with the line's speed as a cap:** off at 566 m, the car 0.3 wide of the line through the whole 450 m bend at steer 0.2–0.3 (the nose points inside the direction of travel by the slip angle, so the heading term steers out); **direction of travel instead of the nose (slip angle counted in full):** 0.20 s faster at 600 m, 0.68 s faster at 2,300 m, then the run ends at 2,351 m (the crest at 286 km/h, no damage logged: the damage stop; v1.10 passes it at 244 km/h); **with a 255 / 245 / 240 km/h cap over the crest:** the run ends at 2,492 m in the Corkscrew, the car 0.6 off the line at full lock and at −0.85 on the right (the line through the Corkscrew cannot be followed over the drop); **zones:** 60–2,150 m: **71.812** (−0.73 s); 2,560–3,200 m alone: 72.452; 2,600–3,560 m or 3,060–3,560 m alone: 74.27 / 74.44 (1.5 s lost at the hairpin: left to the sensor steering); 60–2,250 m: run ends at 2,353 m. **The 70 runs on 60–2,150 m with the slip angle counted in full: 38 of 70 off** (566–1,130 m: a slide is no longer counter-steered: `speedY` −25 → +36 km/h at the 450 m exit, spin at 640 m). **12-run screen** (unperturbed + 11 of the suites' perturbations; means, 0 off unless noted; v1.10 on the same 12 is about 72.56): slip angle limited to 4°: **71.609, 0 off**; nose only: 12 of 12 off; half of the slip angle: 72.712; limit 2 / 3 / 5 / 6°: 72.323 / 71.814 / 71.315 / 71.518; `plan_ffv` 4e-4 / 6e-4 / 8e-4 / 9e-4 / 11e-4 (at 5°): 72.243 / 71.315 / 71.126 / 71.113 / 71.222 (0.993 of the edge on the inside at 1,035 m); `plan_kp` 0.3 / 0.8: 71.632 / 71.714; a position integral: 71.745; | **Decision** | ✅ Kept — 0 damage, 0 of 30 off, shifted bases 0 of 40 off, all-30 70.922 (v1.10 72.555), paired gain over all 70 runs −1.645 s at 96 standard errors with 70 of 70 runs faster, best single lap 1:10.78. Margin: the car now uses 0.69–0.78 of the half-width at every corner inside the zones, by design (the line's limit is 0.65, 0.5 at two apexes, and the worst of the 70 runs is 0.78); every neighbour of the follower's knobs measured stays on track (12-run screens) except the unlimited slip angle (3 of 12 off), two measured steps from the chosen 5°. Spent at the flick, which the line does not steer: the largest of the 70 runs 0.805 → 0.849 (the car arrives from a faster 1,931 m exit). |
| **Learned** | (1) **A planned line pays, and by far more than any single mechanism before it: −1.65 s.** The sensor line used half of the road; the geometry's line through the full width is worth 0.12–0.36 s per corner (1,931 m: slowest point 139 → 170 km/h). (2) **What made it work, in order:** (a) the line's speed must cap the sensor plan (on the line the beams look across the bend and over-read: 122 km/h allowed where the line carries 107); (b) the heading must be measured along the direction of travel, not the nose (on the nose the car sits 0.3 wide of the line in every bend); (c) the slip angle counted must be limited (5°), or a slide is not counter-steered (38 of 70 off without the limit); (d) curvature feed-forward rising with v² (too little: wide of the line, 0.3 s; too much: cuts inside at the fast apexes). (3) **The suites test the planned zones less than they tested the sensor line**: `line_offset`, `line_gain`, `line_aim_off`, `lookahead_gain` do nothing inside a zone, and the car's position there is nearly the same on all 70 runs (worst within 0.03–0.07 of the median). The safety evidence for the zones is therefore the follower's own neighbours (12-run screens above), not the suites: a follower perturbation set (`plan_vs`, `plan_ffv`, `plan_kp`, `plan_slipmax`) should be added to the standard runs. (4) **The line cannot be used everywhere without knowing more than the plan view:** the crest at 2,350 m ends the run above ~255 km/h, the Corkscrew drop cannot be driven on the smooth line (the car is airborne / unloaded), there is a wall at the right edge near 2,490 m, and the hairpin on the line is 1.5 s slower (not traced: probably the line's early apex at full lock and its speed cap against the tuned sensor turn-in). (5) The car cuts inside the line by 0.11–0.19 at the fast right-handers and the 1,261 m kink (feed-forward ∝ v² too strong for bends taken far below the grip limit): handled by a lower line limit at two apexes; a feed-forward keyed to the grip actually used would be cleaner. (6) Traction control keyed to the line's curvature instead of \|steer\| changed nothing (dropped). (7) **Open, in order of estimated gain:** (i) the hairpin and the flick on a planned line (the two slowest corners, 5.9 s and 6.9 s of the lap; est. 0.2–0.5 s; needs elevation and wall data in the tool, a crest speed cap and probably a full-lock model); (ii) speed on the line: the corner table's offsets and `plan_vs` per corner (the model's grip is one fit; `plan_vs` 1.03 measured 0.03 s faster on 12 runs; the car rides the cap with on/off brake touches: a lift band on the cap; est. 0.1–0.3 s); (iii) a lap-time optimiser instead of curvature smoothing (later apexes onto the long straights), and the line limit 0.70–0.75 where the tracking is within 0.05 (limit 0.70 was 0.08 s faster on 12 runs); (iv) the first zone extended to ~2,300 m with a crest cap, the start (0–60 m) and the finish straight; (v) the now inert `line_table` / `turn_table` rows inside the zones can be removed (they still act at 2,150–2,600 m and the hairpin). (8) Races used: about 1,080 (single laps, eight 12-run screens of 2–16 configs, four 70-run sets), in about 50 minutes including the tool. |
